# JudoReplayKit — Project Log

Last updated: 2026-11-17

## Purpose

JudoReplayKit is a portable Windows application intended to help judo referees review match sequences without interrupting the match, live stream, or continuous recording.

The application UI and user-facing documentation are to be written in English. Development discussions may be in Danish.

## Core decisions

- **Replay model:** replay is an overlay on top of the live picture. Replay must be independent of the live stream and recording.
- **Immediate access:** replay should be available with minimal delay, especially for quick confirmation of a referee decision.
- **Continuous recording:** video must be recorded continuously to a user-selected storage location so a sequence can be retrieved later, even if no bookmark was created at the time.
- **Bookmarks:** bookmarks mark moments for later retrieval. Creating a bookmark must not automatically open the bookmark drawer.
- **Production safety:** the known-working production installation must not be changed during testing or repair work. Development changes are isolated in the Rev.8 test installation.

## Installation separation

- Production: `D:\JudoReplayKit` — protected; do not modify during test work.
- Rev.8 test: `D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` — isolated test installation.

## Rev.8 status (2026-11-07)

The isolated Rev.8 test installation has been exercised without changing the production installation.

Verified in the 2026-11-07 session:
- Test web endpoint and all seven configured test ports responded successfully in the recorded check.
- MediaMTX API reported cam1 as `ready=True`, with an RTMP source; cam2 was `ready=False` and had no source, as expected when camera 2 is not sending.
- cam1 recording file was observed at 113,620,191 bytes (about 108 MiB), with LastWriteTime 12:37:36. This followed an earlier observation of the same file at 0 bytes at 12:31:46. This is evidence that the file subsequently grew and recording data was written.
- MediaMTX playback list `http://127.0.0.1:9995/list?path=cam1` returned HTTP 200 and one recording entry: start `2026-11-07T12:31:46.615665+01:00`, duration 636.333 seconds.
- Caddy playback list `http://127.0.0.1:8081/playback/list?path=cam1` also returned HTTP 200. Its returned media URL used `http://127.0.0.1:8081/get?... ` (without the `/playback/` prefix).
- Direct MediaMTX `/get` and Caddy `/playback/get` both returned HTTP 200 with Content-Type `video/mp4`.
- Caddy `/get` without the `/playback/` route returned HTTP 404.
- A read from Caddy `/playback/get` returned 1,024 bytes. The first bytes began `00 00 00 20 66 74 79 70 6D 70 34 32` (`ftypmp42`), consistent with an MP4 container header.
- User tested Instant Replay: the replay window opened but showed no picture. After closing the replay window, the live picture was still present. User also saw a message stating replay does not affect recording. The user's observation does not by itself prove that recording continued during the entire replay-window test.

Still unresolved:
- Instant Replay window opens but displays no video, despite a valid recording being listed and the playback endpoint returning MP4 data.
- The playback list URL generated by Caddy points to `/get`, which returns 404; the working Caddy media route tested was `/playback/get`. Determine whether the replay player uses the generated URL and whether URL rewriting/base-path handling is wrong.
- Replay playback speed/latency and independent overlay behavior have not passed end-to-end testing.
- Live delay was previously reported around 10 seconds and did not match the configured value.
- Pause and frame stepping can snap the playhead back to live.
- Zoom does not reliably follow the selected area.
- Live status indicator can flicker and a false “No signal” overlay has appeared.
- Bookmark creation opens the bookmark drawer automatically.
- Recording filename has a duplicate `.mp4` extension (observed: `2026-11-07_12-31-46-615665.mp4.mp4`).
- Help text should say “PC IP” rather than the ambiguous “IP address”.

## Working rules for contributors and AI assistants

1. Do not modify the production installation.
2. Do not stop or kill running production or Rev.8 services without a clear, approved reason.
3. Do not repeat tests already marked as verified unless a specific new change makes them necessary.
4. Inspect the exact current Rev.8 files before proposing code changes. Do not assume an old file is current.
5. Collect and consolidate defects before editing. Present a repair plan for approval before applying repairs.
6. Distinguish verified facts from hypotheses.
7. Do not publish private chat exports, credentials, tokens, personal data, or match recordings in this public repository.

## Shared AI history and contribution policy (2026-11-07)

The public GitHub repository is the shared historical knowledge base for the user and all AI assistants working on JudoReplayKit. Every assistant must read the relevant repository history before reviewing, diagnosing, or proposing changes; its own chat context is not assumed to be complete.

Contributions are to be recorded with source attribution and date/revision where available. Original analyses, criticism, alternative proposals, disagreements, corrections, and clarifications must remain distinguishable. A later contribution may add to or challenge an earlier one, but must not silently replace it. Summaries must be labelled as summaries and must not be represented as verbatim source material.

AI assistants that cannot write to GitHub can still participate: the user can pass their responses to the designated log maintainer for addition to the repository. Public-repository safety rules remain in force for secrets, credentials, personal data, private network details, and private recordings.

## 2026-11-07 — External AI diagnosis: possible `openInstantReplay()` time-window race

**Source:** Diagnosis supplied by the user's second AI assistant; independently reviewed in this ChatGPT session. **Status: HYPOTHESIS — NOT VERIFIED; repair not approved or applied.**

The second AI identifies the `openInstantReplay()` selection condition `s.end <= now + 350` as a possible cause of the UI message “no completed recording found”. Its proposed explanation is that `now` is captured before asynchronous `/playback/list` requests, so the returned span end may be later than the stale `now + 350` cutoff. It proposes calculating a fresh `Date.now()` after `Promise.all()` resolves and increasing the tolerance to 2,000 ms.

Independent review: the stale-time hypothesis is plausible, but the supplied evidence does not prove it. The previously recorded span start `2026-11-07T12:31:46.615665+01:00` plus duration `1478.433` seconds yields an end around `12:56:25.048+01:00`. That timestamp must be compared with the actual Instant Replay click time and actual list-response time; it cannot be assumed to be in the future. A fresh timestamp after fetch is a reasonable defensive change, but the 2,000 ms tolerance needs justification from measured values and should not mask a clock/time-range or span-selection problem.

**Decision:** Do not approve or apply the code change yet. First capture the actual `/playback/list` response and compare its calculated `s.end` with the browser's `Date.now()` at click time and after the request resolves. Inspect the exact current Rev.8 code and verify how `s.end` is calculated. Production remains protected; no code/configuration or services changed.


## 2026-11-07 — Addendum: browser Network evidence for Instant Replay lookup

**Source:** User-provided browser DevTools Network details and response JSON. **Environment:** Rev.8 test only.

- The browser request to /playback/list?path=cam1 returned HTTP 200.
- Its response contained one cam1 span: start 2026-11-07T12:09:52.409Z, duration 301 seconds, with generated media URL http://localhost:8081/get?... .
- The matching request URL had a query end of 2026-11-07T12:14:53.409Z. Start plus duration is exactly 12:14:53.409Z, matching that requested end.
- The browser request to /playback/list?path=cam2 returned HTTP 404. The separate cam2 HLS playlist request also returned 404. Since cam2 was previously verified as not sending, these cam2 404s are consistent with an offline camera and do not establish a cam1 playback failure.
- The returned cam1 media URL again omits the /playback/ prefix. Caddy's unprefixed /get route was previously verified as 404, while /playback/get returned 200. It is not yet established whether the app consumes this returned URL or constructs its own media URL.
- **Important timing implication:** openInstantReplay() requests a list ending at now + 1000 ms, then accepts only spans with s.end <= now + 350. Here the returned span ends exactly at the requested query end, i.e. apparently now + 1000 ms. If the request's now is the value used to build the URL, this span fails the 350 ms filter by 650 ms. This is strong evidence for the time-window/filter mismatch, rather than merely a generic network-latency race. The precise runtime values have not yet been logged, so retain a small qualification until source/runtime timing is measured.
- The cam1 HTTP 200 response proves the browser receives a non-empty JSON list; it does not alone prove the replay player decodes or displays the media.

**Next:** Inspect the exact current Rev.8 openInstantReplay() and media URL selection path. Confirm whether the span end is compared against the same pre-request now, and whether the player uses the returned url or the app's own /playback/get builder. Do not change code until the user approves a repair plan. Production remains untouched.


## 2026-11-07 — Addendum: independent DeepSeek review of Instant Replay failure

The user supplied an independent technical review from DeepSeek after it reported reading both project logs in full (PROJECT-LOG.md, 238 lines; TEST-LOG.md, 186 lines at the time of its review). DeepSeek independently agreed that the 2,000 ms tolerance proposed earlier was not justified without measurement and that a fresh post-fetch timestamp alone would not reliably resolve the observed boundary mismatch.

### DeepSeek's main conclusions

- It identifies the mismatch between the list request end (captured `now + 1000 ms`) and the acceptance filter (`s.end <= now + 350 ms`). In the supplied cam1 response, the returned span end equals the request end exactly, which places that span 650 ms beyond the acceptance limit if the same captured `now` is used for both.
- It concludes that this arithmetic explains why that returned span is rejected and why the “no completed recording found” branch can be reached.
- It argues that changing only to a fresh `Date.now()` after the fetch is insufficient unless at least 650 ms has elapsed; localhost response time may be shorter.
- It reports that the supplied Rev.8 source excerpts show `listSpans()` discarding the response `url` property and `loadSpan()` using the app's own `playbackUrl()` builder, so the returned `/get` URL is not the current app's media URL path. This makes the Caddy `/get` vs `/playback/get` discrepancy unlikely to explain the current “no completed recording found” branch.

### Independent assessment / qualifications retained

The boundary mismatch is strongly supported by the exact browser request and response already logged. It is mathematically certain that a span ending at requestEnd = capturedNow + 1000 ms fails a filter of capturedNow + 350 ms. However, do not generalize that every response must end at the query boundary: only the supplied cam1 response is observed. The local source excerpts have not yet been independently re-read from the current Rev.8 disk files in this session. Therefore:
- Verified browser fact: this observed span ended exactly at the request end.
- Conditional arithmetic: if the same captured `now` feeds both request construction and filtering, this observed span is rejected by 650 ms.
- Strong diagnosis, not yet full runtime proof: this explains the observed error path.
- Reported code fact, pending current on-disk confirmation: the app ignores response `url` and builds `/playback/get` itself.

DeepSeek proposed two minimal candidate alignments: (A) request list through captured `now` rather than `now + 1000`, preserving the existing +350 ms filter; or (B) keep the request end and align the acceptance threshold to it. It regarded a fresh post-fetch timestamp as supplementary, not the primary fix. No option is approved or implemented.

### Next step

Before any edit, independently inspect the exact current Rev.8 source on disk: `openInstantReplay()`, `listSpans()`, `playbackUrl()`, and the media-loading call site. Then propose a minimal test-only repair plan with explicit rationale and validation. Do not touch production, restart services, change ports/configuration/retention, or refactor unrelated code. No code changed as part of this review.

## 2026-11-07 — Fresh on-disk Rev.8 source confirms request/filter boundary mismatch

The user supplied fresh PowerShell output from the actual file `D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test\app\web\app.js` (61,107 bytes; LastWriteTime 2026-11-06 14:56:44). This is direct source evidence from the isolated Rev.8 test installation.
### Confirmed exact code and line numbers

- Lines 255–268: `listSpans(path,start,end,signal)` requests `/playback/list`, parses each item into only `{ start, end }` using `Date.parse(x.start)` and `Number(x.duration)`; it does not preserve `x.url`.
- Lines 270–275: `playbackUrl(path,start,duration)` builds `/playback/get?...&format=fmp4`.
- Lines 276–284: `mergeSpans()` merges adjacent spans with a 350 ms gap threshold.
- Lines 475–510: `loadSpan()` sets `v.src = playbackUrl(STREAMS[i], span.start, span.end - span.start)` at line 498. The visible excerpt confirms the app builds its own media URL rather than using the list response's `url`.
- Lines 734–765: `openInstantReplay()`.
  - Line 744 captures one `now = Date.now()`.
  - Line 746 passes `new Date(now + 1000)` as the list request end.
  - Line 754 filters using that same captured `now`: `s.end <= now + 350`.
  - Line 756 throws “No completed recording found yet...” if no span passes.
  - Lines 757–759 use `latestEnd` to calculate the requested replay range and call `loadReplay()`.
- Line 1264 binds the Instant Replay button to `openInstantReplay`.
- The broader identifier search also showed `fetchLatestRecordingEnd()` at lines 1136–1144 independently asks through `now + 2000`, then takes the latest span end without the same acceptance filter. This is a separate code path and should not be changed as part of a minimal Instant Replay repair unless evidence requires it.

### Conclusions now directly confirmed

1. The same captured `now` is used in request construction and filtering.
2. Request end is `now + 1000 ms`.
3. Acceptance boundary is `now + 350 ms`.
4. The observed returned span ending exactly at request end is rejected by exactly 650 ms.
5. `listSpans()` discards the response `url` field.
6. `loadSpan()` calls the app's own `playbackUrl()`, which uses `/playback/get`.

This substantially upgrades the time-boundary diagnosis: it is confirmed in current on-disk source, and the previously supplied browser response exhibits exactly the boundary behavior that triggers it. The conditional part remains that this specific response's end must be the one evaluated by this invocation; the observed request/response pair and code make that a strong causal explanation.

### Still to inspect / cautions

The user-provided output includes only the first portion of `loadSpan()` (through line 510), not its complete body. The exact current body of `loadReplay()` and full `loadSpan()` may be useful before final approval, but the current “no completed recording found” branch is reached before `loadReplay()` or media URL loading, so those details are not necessary to establish the boundary mismatch itself.

Do not assume changing request end to `now` is automatically the safest final repair without considering MediaMTX's treatment of an active recording at the exact live edge. Compare the minimal alternatives and recommend a test-only change with clear reasoning. No code changed, no services restarted, production untouched.

## 2026-11-07 — Independent second-AI assessment of Rev.8 Instant Replay boundary mismatch

The user supplied a full independent technical assessment from DeepSeek. DeepSeek explicitly stated it could not access GitHub in that session and had not read either repository log; its assessment was based only on the fresh Rev.8 source excerpts and previously supplied browser Network evidence. This limitation is recorded rather than presenting its review as repository inspection.

DeepSeek independently re-derived the same boundary mismatch:
- openInstantReplay() captures one `now`.
- The /playback/list request ends at `now + 1000 ms`.
- The acceptance filter rejects any span ending after `now + 350 ms`.
- The observed cam1 response ended exactly at the request end, so it falls 650 ms beyond the acceptance boundary.
- When no span passes, the error is thrown before loadReplay() or media loading.
- The response's `url` is discarded by listSpans(); the shown media-loading path builds its own `/playback/get` URL. Thus the Caddy `/get` mismatch does not explain this particular no-span error.

DeepSeek recommends the one-line, isolated Rev.8 candidate: change the list request end from `new Date(now + 1000)` to `new Date(now)`, leaving the existing `s.end <= now + 350` filter unchanged. Its reasoning is that this bounds returned spans at or before the captured client time and avoids widening acceptance into a possible future/unwritten tail. It rejects a fresh Date.now() after fetch as an adequate standalone fix.

Important qualifications retained:
1. The observed request/response pair proves one span ended at the request boundary; it does not establish that MediaMTX always clamps spans this way.
2. Option A is a proposed test-only correction, not yet applied or approved. Active-recording/live-edge behavior and any clock/write lag still require runtime validation.
3. This diagnosis explains the specific “No completed recording found yet” branch; it does not prove all replay/player defects are solved.
4. mergeSpans() 350 ms adjacency tolerance is separate and should not be changed.
5. fetchLatestRecordingEnd() may have a related future-boundary concern because it requests through now + 2000 and takes the latest end without the same filter, but no failure is observed there; do not change it speculatively.

DeepSeek's proposed test remains strictly in D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test, with browser reload and observation of the request, diagnostic lagMs, rendered frames, live picture, and recording-file growth. Production D:\JudoReplayKit must remain untouched. No code changed, no services restarted, and no runtime configuration or retention policy changed during this assessment.

## 2026-11-07 — Rev.8 replay search window excludes stopped-camera recordings

**Source:** User-provided PowerShell output and source inspection. **Environment:** Rev.8 test installation only.

The user observed that Instant Replay once displayed a short sequence with status "Replay loaded", but after a browser refresh it reported "No completed recording found". The user also reported that replay controls seemed unresponsive; scroll-wheel zoom and image panning did work.

Verified source facts:
- `openInstantReplay()` sets `durationSel` from `replayWindowSelect`, defaulting to 15000 ms (15 seconds).
- It sets `lookFrom = now - 5 * 60 * 1000`, so archive discovery is limited to the preceding five minutes.
- The list request uses the captured current time as its end (the previously approved Option A change is present).
- A span is selected only if its end is within the existing acceptance threshold; if none is selected, the code throws "No completed recording found yet" before calling `loadReplay()`.
- The user tested the same five-minute interval at 2026-11-07 13:49:51 +01:00. It covered 13:44:51–13:49:51 and returned HTTP 404.
- The latest CAM1 archive found by the unbounded list request starts at 12:31:46.615665 +01:00 and has duration 4110.145528 seconds, ending at approximately 13:40:16.761. The five-minute query therefore started about 4 minutes 34 seconds after the recorded span ended.
- The unbounded `/list?path=cam1` returned HTTP 200 and the archive span, confirming the recording is still indexed and discoverable.

Conclusion: The five-minute lookback is sufficient to explain why Instant Replay cannot find a completed recording after the camera has been off for more than five minutes. This is a separate limitation from the earlier Option A request-end boundary correction. The 15-second value is the requested replay-window length, not the total archive duration.

Not yet established:
- Whether the first visible replay was exactly 15 seconds.
- Whether play/pause/frame-step controls function.
- Whether replay playback works reliably across the full archive span.
- Whether live streaming and recording remain unaffected during replay.

Recommended next design: Instant Replay should discover the latest available completed span even when the camera is no longer streaming, rather than limiting archive discovery to the last five minutes. Define a safe bounded archive-search horizon or query the archive index for the latest span; avoid loading the entire archive as the replay clip. Any implementation change requires explicit user approval and must be tested only in `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test`. Production `D:\\JudoReplayKit` remains untouched. No code was changed and no services were restarted during this test.

## 2026-11-08 — Independent static review of uploaded Rev.8 source package (ChatGPT)

**Source:** Uploaded archive `JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Review_20261108-120408.zip`; static inspection of the exact included files. **Scope:** Read-only source/configuration review. No local installation, code, configuration, service, or production changes were made.

**Package integrity and contents:**
- ZIP opened successfully and contained 16 entries: 15 project files plus `COLLECTION-REPORT.txt`.
- Included: current `app.js`, two dated `app.js` backups, `index.html`, `hls.min.js`, MediaMTX runtime YAML, Caddyfile and backup, start/stop scripts, README, and TLS certificate/key files.
- MediaMTX and Caddy executables were omitted by the collection script because they exceeded 20 MB. Runtime logs were also excluded. Therefore this review cannot establish binary versions from the archive or correlate source behavior with runtime errors.
- **Security note:** The uploaded archive contains `auto.key` (a private-key file) and `auto.crt`. Their contents are not reproduced or added to this public repository. Do not include private keys in future review archives, public commits, or releases. Assess whether the key is only a local self-signed development key before deciding whether it needs rotation.

**Static source/configuration findings (not runtime test results):**
1. **Cross-platform installation gap:** Rev.8 README explicitly says not to move the test folder because generated configuration contains absolute paths. The MediaMTX `recordPath` points to `C:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test\\Videos\\...`; Caddy's static root points to `D:/JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test/app/web`. The supplied start/stop scripts are PowerShell-specific. No Linux install/start procedure is present in the archive. This conflicts with the user's new top-level Rev.9 requirement for very easy installation on both Windows and Linux.
2. **Recording status is not proof of recording health:** The HTML contains the static note “Continuous recording is configured at the server”. The UI reads `recording-location.txt` to display a path, but the supplied archive does not contain that file. The supplied browser code does not itself verify that recording files are growing or that the recording backend remains healthy. This is a static review observation; backend health might be exposed elsewhere, but it is not demonstrated in this package.
3. **Bookmark persistence and metadata:** Bookmarks are stored in browser `localStorage` under `judoReplayKit.bookmarks.v1`. A new bookmark is assigned `camera: 0`, although the UI supports two cameras; recording-time association is filled asynchronously from the latest span returned across both cameras. This design needs an explicit, stable shared event timestamp and camera/archive association model in Rev.9. The code expands the bookmark drawer immediately after creation, contrary to the recorded product decision that creation must not open the drawer.
4. **Replay seek sequencing:** `waitForVideo()` resolves on `canplay` (or when readyState is already at least 3). `loadSpan()` then assigns `currentTime` and proceeds to set playback rate/start playback without explicitly awaiting the `seeked` event or confirming that the requested frame has been presented. This is a concrete timing/validation weakness worth testing; it is not proof by itself of the user's reported pause/frame-step symptoms.
5. **Frame stepping:** The code uses recorded `requestVideoFrameCallback` timestamps when available, but falls back to an approximate 1/30-second seek if it cannot find a neighboring recorded frame. The UI itself describes the control as approximate. Exact frame-by-frame accuracy is therefore not guaranteed by this implementation.
6. **Archive discovery:** The current uploaded `app.js` contains the 24-hour lookback change and the acceptance filter `s.end <= now + 350`. This confirms the 24-hour discovery expression is in the supplied current file; it does not validate replay rendering or the remaining selection boundary in every runtime condition.
7. **Storage policy:** The supplied test MediaMTX config uses fMP4, one-hour recording segments, and `recordDeleteAfter: 24h`. This is a Rev.8 test configuration, not a final product decision. Retention and disk-full behavior remain undecided for Rev.9.
8. **Network exposure:** The supplied test configuration binds MediaMTX RTMP, HLS, WebRTC, playback, API, and metrics listeners to wildcard addresses. Caddy proxies API and playback routes. Rev.9 must define explicit network exposure, authentication/access controls, and firewall expectations, especially for API/metrics and multi-device tournament LAN use.
9. **Stop-process path check:** The PowerShell stop script checks whether an executable path starts with the test-root string. A path-prefix comparison should use a normalized directory boundary, not only a raw string prefix, before it is relied upon as a safety guarantee. No exploit or process termination was tested.

**Independent review conclusion:** Rev.8 is a useful prototype and contains several good architectural intentions, but it is not yet a validated production-ready system. Do not treat static observations as confirmed runtime causes. Build Rev.9 from an approved requirements specification rather than stacking unverified fixes onto Rev.8.

**Rev.9 requirements direction raised by the user:** The independent review must produce a comprehensive requirements specification. Very easy installation on both Windows and Linux is a fundamental requirement, alongside the already confirmed independent replay overlay, continuous recording, minimal replay delay, compact bookmarks, and strict protection of live streaming/recording from replay operations.

**Safety status:** Production `D:\\JudoReplayKit` and isolated Rev.8 test `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` were not modified. No services were started, stopped, or restarted. Rev.9 has not been created.

## 2026-11-08 — DeepSeek Rev.9 requirements specification received; review pending

**Source:** Complete Rev.9 Requirements Specification pasted by the user from DeepSeek. **Documentation maintained by:** ChatGPT, at the user's explicit request. **Status:** Design proposal only; not approved for implementation.

- DeepSeek supplied a 12-part specification covering executive summary, technical assessment, functional and non-functional requirements, architecture, Windows/Linux installation, storage, UI, security, acceptance tests, risks/open decisions, and implementation roadmap.
- The proposal retains the user's established Option A requirement: replay is an overlay over live video, runs independently, and must not interrupt live streaming or continuous recording. It also includes continuous recording, later sequence retrieval, compact bookmarks, two-camera operation, diagnostics, and easier Windows/Linux installation.
- DeepSeek explicitly states that no code, scripts, configuration, installation, or service changes were made for this specification.
- The specification is **not approved for implementation**. Its eleven listed decisions remain open unless and until the user explicitly approves them. Recommendations in the document must not be recorded as confirmed product decisions.
- A critical internal contradiction requires correction before approval: FR-022/FR-023 require a selected 15-second replay request to cover exactly 15 seconds, but Part 5.3 also permits requesting the full archive span and its sample `loadSpan()` flow builds the URL from `span.start` and `span.duration`. The specification must define one unambiguous request contract and align the requirements, architecture, implementation pseudocode, and AT-023/AT-080.
- The specification's proposed bookmark persistence is incomplete at architecture level: it proposes a server-side `bookmarks.json` as the source of truth, but does not identify a concrete server API/component that safely reads/writes it, handles concurrent clients, performs atomic updates, and enforces access control. A browser-only UI cannot directly write a host file.
- Network defaults need a more precise split: localhost-only binding for the UI/API/metrics is not the same as RTMP camera ingest. Phones publishing RTMP over the tournament LAN cannot reach an RTMP listener bound only to 127.0.0.1. The specification must separately define RTMP ingress binding/firewall rules and management/UI/API exposure.
- The recording filename recommendation (omit `.mp4` from `recordPath` because MediaMTX appends it) must be verified against the exact pinned MediaMTX v1.21.3 behavior and a test before being treated as a Rev.9 design fact; Rev.8 actually showed a duplicate `.mp4.mp4` suffix.
- Other items requiring clarification include: the relationship between bookmark click time and post-roll availability; how two independent cameras achieve the proposed ±500 ms synchronization; the missing bookmark API and concurrency model; the feasibility/meaning of “stop cleanly with no partial write” on disk-full; and the basis for proposed CPU/RAM/latency targets.
- The unexplained 25.001-second request discrepancy remains a mandatory Rev.9 investigation item. It is not resolved by the specification and must not be marked fixed or explained.
- The changed Caddy `header_down Location` directive remains unexplained and must be tested as specified; no conclusion has been reached about whether it is required.

**Next gate:** Ask DeepSeek to revise the contradictory/incomplete parts and return a corrected specification. Then review the revised document and obtain the user's explicit approval for each open product decision before implementation. No Rev.9 implementation work is authorized by receipt of the specification alone.

**Safety status:** This is a documentation/review entry only. No source code, scripts, configuration, services, production installation, or Rev.8 test installation were changed.

## 2026-11-08 — Gemini peer review received; implementation-readiness recommendation requested from all three reviewers

**Source:** Complete Gemini peer-review comments pasted by the owner. **Documentation maintained by:** ChatGPT at the owner's request. **Status:** Peer-review input received; no Rev.9 revision or implementation approved.

### Gemini's peer-review conclusions

- **Five-minute vs. 24-hour archive lookback:** Gemini explicitly withdraws its earlier five-minute statement as referring to pre-correction notes. It accepts that ChatGPT's static inspection of the latest supplied Rev.8 review ZIP (`JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Review_20261108-120408.zip`) found the current `app.js` using a 24-hour lookback. This resolves the reviewer disagreement at the source-review level, subject to recording/verifying the exact package hash and ensuring runtime tests use that same package.
- **fMP4 initialization / `moov` hypothesis:** Gemini agrees this is only a hypothesis. It requires inspection of actual HTTP byte-range requests/responses and browser media/decoder diagnostics before deciding whether a custom MSE parser is needed.
- **JudoCore supervisor:** Gemini accepts that a compiled supervisor is only one possible solution and adds complexity. It agrees a simpler launcher that generates/uses relative paths should be tested as a proof of concept before adopting a new supervisor.
- **Historical HTTP tests and performance targets:** Gemini says the historical HTTP observations came from earlier user-provided PowerShell output and are not formal acceptance evidence. They should be reproduced and logged in the isolated test environment. Retention thresholds and deletion rules, including a 10 GB free-space threshold, remain proposals only; destructive behavior requires explicit owner approval.
- **Camera synchronization and frame stepping:** Gemini agrees that a shared server UTC clock does not synchronize independent phone capture/encoder clocks. It also agrees that waiting for a browser `seeked` event does not guarantee exact frame stepping with variable frame rates. Both require empirical measurement.
- Gemini endorses the comparison's evidence-first sequence and says no final requirements specification should be revised before critical evidence, particularly playback payload/decoder behavior and time-boundary behavior, is collected through safe read-only tests in `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test`.

### Remaining gaps in Gemini's response

Gemini did not directly resolve or explain:
- The observed browser playback request duration of 25.001 seconds while the UI selection was 15 seconds.
- The unexplained Caddy `header_down Location ^/ /hls/` difference between the current Caddyfile and its dated backup.
- The concrete arithmetic risk between the list request end (`now + 1000 ms`) and the span acceptance filter (`s.end <= now + 350 ms`).
- All 15 items in ChatGPT's preliminary review were not individually answered.

These remain open investigation/review items; Gemini's silence is not evidence that they are resolved.

### Consolidated status after Gemini peer review

- The earlier five-minute lookback claim is withdrawn; the latest supplied Rev.8 source package is reported to contain a 24-hour lookback.
- The boundary/filter mismatch remains strongly supported by the previously logged browser request/response arithmetic, but still needs exact runtime logging against the identified source package.
- The 25.001-second duration discrepancy and Caddy `header_down` change remain unexplained.

## 2026-11-08 — Three-reviewer implementation-readiness decision: CONDITIONAL GO for read-only diagnostics only

**Sources:** The owner supplied the complete implementation-readiness recommendations from DeepSeek and Gemini. ChatGPT's independent recommendation was already given in the project conversation. **Documentation maintained by:** ChatGPT, at the owner's request. **Status:** Review decision and next-step recommendation; no new test has been run and no implementation is approved.

### Consolidated decision

All three reviewers converge on the same practical boundary:

- **CONDITIONAL GO** for a narrowly scoped, read-only diagnostic phase in the isolated Rev.8 test environment.
- **NO-GO** for full Rev.9 application coding or treating the current Rev.9 specification as an approved implementation baseline.
- No reviewer recommends beginning a broad rewrite, new application architecture, supervisor, persistence layer, or installer implementation now.

The term “implementation” must not blur three different levels:
1. **N1 — Read-only diagnostics:** inspect and record existing files, hashes, browser requests/responses, media state, and runtime behavior without changing files or services. Approved as the next activity.
2. **N2 — Isolated proof of concept:** a disposable, separate experiment for one defined technical question. Not approved yet; first complete the relevant N1 diagnostics and obtain the owner's explicit approval.
3. **N3 — Full Rev.9 implementation:** application architecture, product code, persistence, supervisor, installers, and cross-platform build. Not approved; blocked by unresolved Rev.8 behavior, specification gaps, and owner decisions.

### DeepSeek recommendation (as supplied by the owner)

DeepSeek gave **CONDITIONAL GO for N1 only**, and NO-GO for N2 until the relevant diagnostics are complete and for N3 until the blockers are resolved. It identifies the following high-priority unresolved matters:
- The observed 25.001-second playback request while the UI selection was 15 seconds.
- The unexplained Caddy `header_down Location ^/ /hls/` directive.
- The fMP4 initialization/moov hypothesis, which requires actual payload and decoder evidence.
- Runtime verification of the request-end / span-acceptance boundary behavior.
- Replay video disappearance and the pause/seek/frame-step behavior.

DeepSeek also identifies specification corrections needed before implementation: the FR-022/FR-023 versus Part 5.3 replay-duration contract; a concrete server-side bookmark API and concurrency/atomic-write model; separate RTMP-ingress and management/UI network-binding rules; verification of MediaMTX v1.21.3 filename behavior; a credible two-camera synchronization mechanism; and realistic disk-full/recovery behavior. Retention, deletion, platform support, installer technology, network exposure, authentication, clock policy, Caddy directive, and video preload remain owner decisions or unresolved design questions.

DeepSeek's proposed first sequence is E1 (source identity/hash check), then E2/E3 (duration and media-delivery diagnostics), followed by the remaining behavioral tests. It explicitly says that an unexpected E1 version result should stop the sequence pending source identification.

### Gemini recommendation (as supplied by the owner)

Gemini gave **NO-GO for full application coding** and **CONDITIONAL GO for targeted PoC/read-only diagnostics**. It independently identifies the playback/decode mismatch, time-boundary behavior, 25.001-second discrepancy, Caddy directive provenance, and launcher-versus-supervisor decision as unresolved.

Gemini's proposed Test A inspects the actual playback payload and MP4 atom structure. Its proposed Test B suggests adding console logging to `app.js`; that would modify the test installation and is therefore **not included in the approved read-only phase**. If additional instrumentation later proves necessary, it must be proposed as a separate, minimal test-only change and explicitly approved by the owner first.

Gemini also agrees that a simpler launcher proof of concept should be evaluated before assuming a compiled JudoCore supervisor is necessary. Neither supervisor nor launcher architecture is approved at this point.

### ChatGPT recommendation

ChatGPT's recommendation is **CONDITIONAL GO for read-only diagnostics and NO-GO for Rev.9 coding**, matching the common position of DeepSeek and Gemini.

ChatGPT recommends beginning with **E1 only**: identify and hash the exact files in the running/reviewed Rev.8 test package, including `app.js`, `index.html`, Caddy configuration, and MediaMTX runtime configuration, and record the relevant MediaMTX version. If the installed/tested files do not match the package/version under review, stop and reconcile the source identity before proceeding.

After E1 is confirmed, proceed to E2 and E3: observe the selected replay duration against the actual browser request and inspect playback response headers, range behavior, payload structure, and browser media/decoder state. Do not download an unnecessarily large recording body. Then plan E4–E6 for pause/seek/frame-step, two-camera synchronization, and recording continuity during replay, using observation only and without restarting services.

### Consolidated blockers and evidence discipline

The following remain unresolved and must not be described as fixed:
- The 25.001-second request versus 15-second UI selection.
- Whether the returned fMP4 data is complete and successfully decoded/presented; HTTP 200, Content-Type, or `readyState: 4` alone do not prove visible playback.
- The cause and necessity of the Caddy `header_down Location` directive.
- Runtime behavior after the Rev.8 Option A time-window change.
- Replay disappearance, pause, seeking, frame stepping, and return-to-live behavior.
- Actual two-camera synchronization.
- Recording continuity during concurrent replay.
- The duplicate `.mp4.mp4` filename and exact MediaMTX filename semantics.

The current Rev.9 specification remains a useful review proposal, **not an implementation contract**. Its contradictions, incomplete interfaces, unverified assumptions, and owner decisions must be resolved and recorded before N3. Recommendations from reviewers are not product decisions. No proposed retention/deletion threshold, authentication policy, performance target, network default, installer technology, or supervisor architecture is approved by this entry.

### Approved next action and safety boundary

Do not proceed to E2 until E1 confirms which package is actually being examined. If identity is inconsistent or uncertain, stop and report the discrepancy.

For this initial diagnostic phase:

## 2026-11-08 — Follow-up reviewer clarifications: strengthen E1, observe time-dependent playback, preserve staged fallback

**Sources:** Additional comments from DeepSeek and Gemini supplied by the owner after the three-reviewer readiness entry above. **Status:** Clarification of the approved diagnostic approach; no tests have been run and no files/services were changed.

### Consensus reaffirmed

DeepSeek confirms agreement with the CONDITIONAL GO for read-only diagnostics and NO-GO for full Rev.9 coding. Gemini explicitly retracts its earlier suggestion to add `console.log()` lines to the original `app.js` during the initial diagnostic phase and now endorses the read-only DevTools approach. The earlier entry's description of Gemini's Test B is therefore superseded by this clarification: **no instrumentation is approved in the Rev.8 test installation**.

DeepSeek also clarifies that its approach and Gemini's intended fallback are sequential rather than opposing strategies: begin with read-only observation; only if that is insufficient, consider instrumentation in a separate copy, with explicit owner approval. No such copy or instrumentation is currently authorized.

### Refinements to E1–E3

1. **E1 must verify both identity and relevant content.** In addition to SHA-256 hashes for `app.js`, `index.html`, Caddyfile, and `mediamtx-runtime.yml`, record MediaMTX version and use read-only text searches to locate the known expressions/markers:
   - `lookFrom = now - 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000`
   - `new Date(now)`
   - `s.end <= now + 350`
   - `header_down Location`
   
   Hashes establish file identity; content inspection establishes whether the expected code/configuration markers are actually present. If E1 identifies an unexpected or inconsistent package/version, stop and reconcile it before proceeding.

2. **E2 must include time-dependent observation.** In addition to the selected dropdown value, localStorage, request URL, response and media-element state, observe replay behavior over approximately 60–120 seconds. This specifically addresses the reported symptom where video disappeared after some time and returned after a hard refresh. Record relevant visible behavior and browser/media state without changing the application.

3. **E3 remains payload/transport inspection.** Record status, Content-Type, Content-Length or transfer behavior, range/206 behavior, and safely inspect the relevant MP4 initialization/fragment structure. Avoid downloading a large recording unnecessarily. HTTP 200 or `readyState: 4` alone is not proof that video is visibly decoded and presented.

4. **E1–E3 may be performed in one diagnostic session** if E1 confirms the expected package and no discrepancy requires investigation. The sequencing gate remains: E2/E3 must not proceed against an unidentified or unexpected build. This refines the previous recommendation to do E1 first; it does not require waiting days between read-only observations.

### Priority clarification: Caddy directive

The presence or absence of `header_down Location ^/ /hls/` should be recorded during E1. Its rationale and effect remain undocumented and should be investigated later alongside an HLS regression check (including the proposed AT-081), but DeepSeek considers it lower priority than the active replay/playback issues while HLS live is working. It is an unresolved configuration question, not currently established as a live blocker.

### E7 — conditional diagnostic fallback only

If E1–E3 do not explain the 25.001-second discrepancy or another specific runtime question, a later fallback may be proposed:

- Create a separate diagnostic copy, e.g. `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test_Diag\\`, rather than editing the protected Rev.8 test installation.
- Add only the minimum required instrumentation to that copy.
- Clearly identify which shared services/data the copy would contact and assess any risk of interference before running it.
- Obtain the owner's explicit approval before creating the copy, editing files, or running the instrumented build.

This is **not approved now**. It is a possible E7 proposal only if read-only evidence is insufficient. The original production and Rev.8 test installations remain protected.

### Updated next-step sequence

- **E1:** Read-only hashes, version identity, and targeted content-marker inspection.
- **E2 + E3:** If E1 matches the expected build, run the duration/request and media-payload/browser diagnostics in the same session; include 60–120 seconds of observation for video disappearance.

- **Rev.9:** Remains NO-GO for full coding until technical blockers, specification contradictions/incomplete interfaces, and owner decisions are resolved.

### Reviewer follow-up and E2/E3 protocol

Gemini and DeepSeek both endorse proceeding to read-only browser diagnostics after E1. DeepSeek highlights the need to establish whether the previously reported `duration=25.001` can be reproduced by the current build, rather than assuming the old observation is representative. That discrepancy remains open.

To avoid relying on unverified assumptions, the next browser session must use the actual Rev.8 test page/address already in use; do not assume an unverified URL, query flag, dropdown element ID, or video element ID. Do not restart the test application or services merely to begin this observation.

**E2 — duration and time-dependent playback observation:**
- In browser DevTools, enable Network Preserve log and clear the existing request list.
- Set the UI duration to 15 seconds and activate Instant Replay once.
- Capture the actual request URL(s), especially the full `playback/list` and `playback/get` URLs and query parameters, along with status and response details.
- Record the selected UI value and whether the actual request duration is 15, 25.001, the full archive span, or another value.
- Observe the replay for 60–120 seconds and note whether video becomes black/disappears, whether the UI reports an error, and whether it recovers without refresh.
- Keep the normal camera/live conditions unchanged for the first observation so the test reflects normal operating conditions. If a later isolation test requires disabling a camera, propose it separately and record that it changes the test conditions.
- Inspect Console errors and browser media state only after identifying the actual relevant page elements; do not assume element IDs from an external reviewer suggestion.

**E3 — playback transport/payload evidence:**
- For the actual media request, record HTTP status, Content-Type, Content-Length or transfer behavior, and Content-Range / 206 behavior if present.
- Inspect browser-visible media/decoder/presentation evidence. HTTP 200, `Content-Type: video/mp4`, or `readyState=4` alone does not prove that decoded frames were visibly presented.
- Safely inspect available MP4 initialization/fragment evidence (`ftyp`, `moov`, `moof`) without downloading a large recording unnecessarily.

E2 and E3 may be completed in one session because E1 matched the expected markers and MediaMTX version. If the page/build identity is contradicted by browser evidence, stop and reconcile it before interpreting further results.

### Open observations retained

- The 25.001-second request versus the 15-second UI selection remains unexplained.
- The earlier boundary arithmetic between list request end (`now + 1000 ms`) and acceptance filter (`s.end <= now + 350 ms`) remains a strong candidate requiring runtime evidence.
- The Caddy `header_down Location ^/ /hls/` directive is confirmed present. Its purpose/effect remains unknown and is lower priority while HLS live is working; record it for later HLS regression analysis.
- The fMP4 initialization/moov issue remains a hypothesis pending actual payload and browser decoder evidence.
- No repair, instrumentation, isolated copy, or Rev.9 implementation is authorized by this entry.

## 2026-11-08 — Strategic clarification: Rev.9 requirements specification is now the main workstream

**Source:** Owner clarification in the JudoReplayKit project conversation. **Status:** Workstream correction and authorization boundary; no code or installation changes.

### Corrected direction

The intended next deliverable is a **new, complete Rev.9 requirements specification for an application to be implemented from scratch**. The work is not to continue repairing, patching, or incrementally extending Rev.8.

The earlier wording that made E2/E3 the immediate next task was misleading. E2/E3 are optional, supplemental, read-only investigations. They may provide useful evidence and help formulate acceptance tests, but **they are not prerequisites for drafting Rev.9 and must not delay the specification**.

The existing Rev.8 findings are to be treated as:
- documented observations and technical risks;
- examples of failure modes that Rev.9 must prevent;
- inputs to concrete acceptance tests;
- hypotheses only where the cause has not been established.

They are not a list of Rev.8 repairs to implement.

### Agreed sequence from this clarification

1. Draft the complete Rev.9 requirements specification, distinguishing confirmed owner requirements, derived technical requirements, unresolved owner decisions, Rev.8 observations/hypotheses, and measurable acceptance tests.
2. Share the draft with DeepSeek and Gemini for independent review.
3. Consolidate disagreements, contradictions, missing interfaces, and decisions requiring the owner's choice.
4. Revise the document and obtain the owner's explicit approval of the specification as the implementation baseline.
5. Only after approval, prepare the implementation plan and begin a clean Rev.9 implementation from scratch.

### Boundaries retained

- No Rev.8 repair or modification is authorized.
- No Rev.9 application coding, installer work, supervisor implementation, or isolated PoC is authorized by this clarification.
- E2/E3 remain optional read-only diagnostics and may be performed later if their evidence would materially improve a specific requirement or acceptance test.
- Production `D:\\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected. No services are to be started, stopped, or restarted as part of this specification work.

**Next deliverable:** Rev.9 — complete requirements specification, draft for independent review; not yet an approved implementation contract.

## 2026-11-08 — Independent review comparison: Gemini and DeepSeek Rev.9 proposals

**Source:** Complete reviewer texts supplied by the owner in the project conversation. **Reviewer attribution:** Gemini and DeepSeek. **Additional assessment:** ChatGPT. **Status:** Review findings only; no specification approval and no implementation authorization.

### Overall assessment

- DeepSeek supplied the more complete and test-oriented of the two reviewer proposals. Its strengths are explicit requirement IDs, a more detailed bookmark/persistence contract, clearer failure states, a broad acceptance-test matrix, and explicit acknowledgement of technical limitations.
- Gemini supplied useful architecture and operational observations, especially component separation, recording-health evidence, failure containment, and the suggestion to evaluate a lightweight launcher before committing to a compiled supervisor.
- Neither proposal should replace REV9-REQUIREMENTS-DRAFT.md draft 0.1 or be adopted unchanged. The existing draft 0.1 remains the better base because it is more technology-neutral and deliberately leaves unapproved technical choices and numeric targets open.
- The reviewer texts are proposals, not owner decisions. Their suggested thresholds, protocols, formats, storage policies and architectures are not approved.

### DeepSeek proposal — strengths and issues to resolve

**Useful contributions to incorporate or evaluate:**
- Make the selected replay interval contract explicit and test UI selection, requested interval, returned media interval and actually presented interval separately.
- Define bookmark persistence as a server-owned service/API with stable identifiers, validation, atomicity/transactions, concurrency and recovery.
- Give replay, archive and bookmark failures distinct machine-readable states rather than a generic failure.
- Explicitly test archive discovery when a camera is offline, interrupted recording, post-roll pending/partial states, long-running sessions and two-client bookmark writes.
- State honestly that browser frame stepping may be approximate unless the chosen media pipeline proves exact decoded-frame stepping.
- Measure two-camera offset and drift rather than assuming a common PC UTC clock guarantees capture synchronization.
- Keep camera ingest network exposure separate from UI, management and metrics exposure.

**Corrections/caveats:**
1. The proposed duration formula and requirement for exactly one request with duration=15.000 are too prescriptive before the actual archive API/media semantics are selected. A media container's reported duration may differ from the requested/presented interval. Specify the user-visible interval and a measurable presentation tolerance first; then choose whether exact-range retrieval or larger-span retrieval plus trimming is the implementation contract.
2. The requirement that video.duration equal the requested duration within ±0.1 s is not by itself a valid proof of correct interval or visible decode. It must be paired with timestamp/decoded-frame evidence and visible presentation.
3. The 30 s pre-roll + 15 s post-roll bookmark rule is not approved. Earlier discussions considered 30 s before and approximately 10–15 s after, but the final behavior remains an owner decision.
4. clickTimeMs, server time, recording-span end and capture time are not interchangeable. The bookmark schema must define the time source and mapping to each camera's archive timeline; a server timestamp alone does not establish the exact captured frame time.
5. Storing a single bookmarks.json in the recording folder and using temp/fsync/rename is one possible design, not a settled architecture. Filesystem semantics, concurrent writers, backups, removable drives and database recovery must be evaluated before selecting it.
6. The proposed ±5 s tolerance, 500 ms seek response, 8 s seek timeout, 15 s decode timeout, exact 1/30-second step semantics and other numeric thresholds need a reference platform and measurement method. Do not turn them into binding targets without owner approval.
7. The frame-step acceptance test allowing either 20 visibly different frames or an APPROXIMATE label is not sufficient. The test must verify the declared stepping behavior and label honestly; an approximate label cannot substitute for a working control.
8. The synchronization proposal should not assume that a manual visible marker automatically records each phone's actual capture-frame timestamp. The mechanism must be technically defined and validated; otherwise describe it as a calibration aid, not guaranteed synchronization.
9. The proposed support matrix, Basic authentication, localhost defaults, portable ZIP and sidecar choice are proposals only.

### Gemini proposal — strengths and issues to resolve

**Useful contributions to incorporate or evaluate:**
- Separate responsibilities for ingest, live delivery, recording/archive, replay, bookmark persistence, UI and lifecycle management.
- Require recording-health evidence based on actual write progress, not merely an open port or configured path.
- Require replay failures to be contained so they cannot interrupt recording or ingest.
- Evaluate a simple launcher before deciding that a compiled Supervisor is needed.
- Keep disk-full behavior technically honest and do not promise that an in-progress file can never be partial.

**Corrections/caveats:**
1. Gemini's statement that the core conflict is specifically HLS/WebRTC plus fMP4 writes is not established as a product requirement. Rev.9 must compare media/transport options rather than inherit Rev.8 technologies.
2. fMP4 does not, by itself, guarantee recovery up to the second after sudden power loss. Segment finalization, fragment/index structure, write buffering and recovery behavior must be specified and tested.
3. The proposed 1-second replay opening target and 3-second live-delay target are not approved. “Replay available” must be defined as first visibly presented frame, and tested on named reference hardware/network/browser conditions.
4. The proposal to bind both RTMP ingest and the UI proxy to 0.0.0.0 is unsafe as a blanket rule and conflicts with the need to restrict management exposure. Each endpoint/interface must have an explicit exposure policy. The example RTMP port 1936 is not established; Rev.8 used 1935, but neither port is mandated for Rev.9.
5. Automatic deletion of the oldest recording below 10 GB and default 30-day retention are destructive proposals and are explicitly unapproved. They must not be adopted without an owner-approved deletion policy, protected/locked tournament rules and recovery safeguards.
6. A compiled Supervisor, Node/Python sidecar, Caddy and MediaMTX are candidate components, not approved architecture. Their need and responsibilities must be justified against simpler alternatives.
7. The proposed storage-recovery acceptance test that fills a disk to 99% and expects automatic deletion is unsafe until deletion policy is explicitly approved.

### Consolidated findings for draft 0.2

The next revision should preserve the technology-neutral structure of draft 0.1 and strengthen it in these areas:

- Add an explicit end-to-end replay timing contract separating click time, selected interval, archive availability, request interval, media/container interval, first decoded frame, first visibly presented frame and return-to-live.
- Define bookmark time semantics and the relationship between event time, server time, camera capture time, ingest time and archive timestamps.
- Add logical API/data-contract requirements without selecting a particular language, database or process topology.
- Define component health as distinct signals: camera connected, frames arriving, live frames presented, recording writes progressing, archive query available, replay decoded/presented, storage healthy.
- Add failure containment and recovery behavior for every logical component.
- Strengthen two-camera synchronization requirements to measure offset and drift using a defined reference event and disclose confidence/uncertainty.
- Define honest frame-step capability levels and matching UI language; do not accept a label alone as a substitute for functional behavior.
- Add test environment metadata and evidence requirements to every acceptance-test report.
- Review numeric thresholds only after defining reference hardware, browser, camera configuration, network conditions and measurement points.
- Keep retention, deletion, authentication, supported OS matrix, transport, recording format, installer, supervisor/launcher and database as OPEN until the owner decides or an architecture comparison justifies a proposal.

### Decision and safety status

- REV9-REQUIREMENTS-DRAFT.md draft 0.1 remains the working baseline for specification refinement, not an approved implementation contract.
- The Gemini and DeepSeek proposals are retained as attributed review inputs; neither is adopted wholesale.
- No owner decisions have been made by this review.
- No Rev.8 diagnostics, repairs, Rev.9 code, PoC, installer, configuration or service changes were performed.
- Production D:\JudoReplayKit and Rev.8 test D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test remain protected.

**Next step:** Prepare draft 0.2 by applying the agreed clarifications above, preserving unresolved choices as OPEN, then return it for owner review. Do not begin implementation until the owner explicitly approves a numbered specification.
