- New content/blob SHA: `c71035e6f599e6c2d76becd8e762a4ebea5c7745`
- The updated file was fetched again after the write; its draft 0.2 heading and normative-addendum marker were verified.

### Draft 0.2 changes
- Adds an end-to-end replay timing contract distinguishing click time, requested interval, archive availability, API/request interval, actual media/container interval, first decoded frame, first visibly presented frame and return-to-live.
- Clarifies the selected-duration contract: correctness is judged by the visible logical interval, not container duration or request size alone.
- Separates event time, server receipt/persistence time, camera capture time, ingest time and archive time, and requires explicit per-camera mapping method/uncertainty for bookmarks.
- Adds logical API/data-contract expectations for inputs, outputs, validation, errors, idempotency, concurrency and persistence without selecting routes, language, database or process topology.
- Expands health into independent evidence-based states: camera connected, frames arriving, live frames presented, recording writes progressing, archive query available, replay decoded, replay visibly presented and storage healthy.
- Adds component-by-component failure containment/recovery requirements.
- Strengthens two-camera synchronization measurement to include clock offset, drift, actual frame alignment and uncertainty; a shared UTC clock or manual marker alone is not proof.
- Clarifies exact versus best-effort frame-step semantics; a label cannot substitute for a functioning control.
- Adds reference test-environment and evidence requirements, including decoder and visible-presentation evidence.
- Adds AT-084 through AT-090 for replay timing trace, bookmark mapping, health independence, API concurrency/idempotency, failure containment, sync uncertainty and frame-step truthfulness.
- Preserves unapproved choices as OPEN: retention/deletion, disk-full behavior, platform matrix, numeric performance thresholds, media transport/container, authentication, installer, launcher/supervisor, database and other architecture choices.

### FR-022/FR-023 versus Part 5.3 traceability note
The fetched draft 0.1 contained no stable FR identifiers and its Section 5 was “Continuous recording and archive”; it did not contain the cited FR-022/FR-023 and Part 5.3 duration text. Draft 0.2 therefore does **not** claim that the historical conflict is resolved. It records the reference as an unresolved source-traceability item and asks reviewers/owner to identify the original revision/text. New numbering must not be conflated with the older reference.

### Review and safety status
- Draft 0.2 is not approved and is not an implementation contract.
- It is ready to be returned to the owner for review, then supplied unchanged to Gemini and DeepSeek for independent review.
- No code, PoC, installer, configuration or service work was performed.
- Production `D:\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected and untouched.
- No Rev.8 diagnostic session was run or changed.

**Next step:** Owner reviews the exact draft 0.2. If accepted for external review, provide the same GitHub version to Gemini and DeepSeek and retain their reports separately with attribution. No implementation before explicit owner approval of a final numbered specification.


## 2026-11-08 — Consolidated independent review of Rev.9 draft 0.2 (Gemini + DeepSeek)

**Sources:** Complete Gemini and DeepSeek review reports supplied by the owner in the project conversation; exact repository copies of `REV9-REQUIREMENTS-DRAFT.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and this project log fetched from the GitHub default branch. **Additional assessment:** ChatGPT. **Status:** Consolidated review record and preparation for Draft 0.3; no owner decisions, no specification approval and no implementation authorization.

### Repository verification and correction of a reviewer claim

- The exact current `REV9-REQUIREMENTS-DRAFT.md` is Draft 0.2, blob SHA `c71035e6f599e6c2d76becd8e762a4ebea5c7745`.
- The exact current `DECISIONS.md` was fetched and inspected (blob SHA `d1ee45a432133d2bff2dea8c5b5bd83e7dd85471`).
- **DeepSeek's claim that DECISIONS.md confirms replay-duration choices 10/15/20/30 seconds is not supported by the current file.** The fetched file contains no such list. Draft 0.2's available duration choices and default therefore remain OPEN unless another authoritative owner decision/source is produced.
- The confirmed decisions in DECISIONS.md remain: replay overlay (Option A) independent of live/recording; continuous recording to user-selected tournament storage; compact bookmark control that does not auto-open its drawer; green Live state and “PC IP” wording; easy installation on Windows and Linux as a product requirement; production/test separation.
- The project log itself was fetched before update at blob SHA `1c748c5f0b2473186b63b4d175c42747e9170690`.

### Common conclusion

Both independent reviewers conclude that Draft 0.2 is suitable for continued specification/review work but **not** an implementation baseline. ChatGPT agrees. No coding or implementation work should begin.

### Consolidated findings to address or explicitly defer

1. **Replay contract and measurable timing:** Resolve the exact user-visible interval contract (exact-range retrieval versus larger-span retrieval with explicit trim), distinguish requested/available/media/presented intervals, and define how tests prove first visibly presented requested frames. Keep performance numbers provisional until a reference environment and measurement method are approved.
2. **Frame-step semantics:** Owner must choose exact decoded-frame stepping or a clearly specified best-effort temporal step. UI claims and acceptance tests must match actual capability; a label alone is not sufficient.
3. **Bookmarks and API/data contracts:** Specify logical bookmark schema, stable IDs, event/capture/ingest/server/archive time mapping, persistence guarantees, concurrency/idempotency, validation, errors and recovery. Do not prematurely mandate JSON, SQLite, a particular API route, filesystem primitive or process topology.
4. **Archive and recording lifecycle:** Specify archive query outcomes, segment completion/incompletion, interrupted shutdown recovery, camera reconnect identity/session behavior, removable storage loss and tournament close/reopen semantics.
5. **Storage and I/O resilience:** Add requirements for slow/contended storage and safe disk-full testing. Define observable handling of incomplete tail segments and storage failure. No destructive deletion or automatic retention policy without explicit owner approval.
6. **Network/security:** Define exposure and authorization requirements per endpoint category (camera ingest, UI, replay delivery, management API, metrics/diagnostics). Do not adopt blanket interface bindings or authentication technology without review/owner approval.
7. **Two-camera synchronization:** Specify a reproducible measurement/calibration method, offset/drift/uncertainty reporting and criteria for “aligned” versus “approximate”. A common host UTC clock or a visible marker alone does not prove capture-frame synchronization.
8. **Health/diagnostics:** Define evidence fields, freshness/timeout semantics, log rotation/retention/export and privacy-safe diagnostics.
9. **Platform/UI/lifecycle:** Define supported display dimensions/orientations and touch usability; establish Windows/Linux support matrix and lifecycle expectations before the relevant acceptance tests are treated as executable.
10. **Tests and test safety:** Classify tests as (a) runnable with current requirements, (b) blocked by Gate A owner decisions, or (c) requiring a prototype/reference environment. Each test needs a reproducible procedure, safe disposable test environment, evidence and pass/fail criteria. Disk-full tests must not fill a host/system disk.
11. **Additional cases:** Consider bookmark export/import, multiple browser tabs/clients, multi-tournament and multi-mat scope, DST/time-zone transitions, physical storage removal, camera identity replacement, application close/crash/restart and log handling. Each must be specified or explicitly deferred with rationale.

### Reviewer-specific observations

**Gemini:** Useful findings include I/O isolation/resilience, application close/restart state, display support, network-loss bookmark behavior, incomplete media handling, camera reconnect contracts, test automation/evidence, safe disk-full testing, licensing and early security/multi-mat decisions. Its suggestions about mandatory write priority, IndexedDB exclusion, MSE/native playback limitations and capture-time synchronization should be treated as technical hypotheses/options to evaluate, not automatic requirements.

**DeepSeek:** Useful findings include separating runnable/decision-blocked/prototype-dependent tests, specifying health freshness and archive/bookmark error contracts, reproducible failure injection, safe storage test environments, lifecycle and log requirements, and clarifying that several tests depend on Gate A decisions. Its recommendations to mandate particular persistence mechanisms, atomic-write primitives, API status codes, calibration mechanisms or architecture choices are candidates for evaluation, not settled decisions.

### Gate A candidate decisions — owner review required

The following appear likely to block a coherent baseline and should be prioritized for owner discussion:
- User-visible replay duration contract and available duration choices/default.
- Frame-step capability promise.
- Bookmark persistence guarantees and logical contract (without necessarily selecting implementation technology).
- Disk-full and removable-storage failure behavior, including the boundary on any data deletion.
- Endpoint exposure/authentication policy.
- Required two-camera alignment target and acceptable uncertainty.
- Supported operating systems/distributions, browser and reference hardware needed to make acceptance criteria testable.
- Whether multi-mat operation is in the initial Rev.9 scope or only a future extensibility requirement.

Other matters (for example log retention period, bookmark export format, detailed UI layout, audio, zoom limits and multi-tournament workflows) may be post-prototype or later decisions only if the specification explicitly records the deferral and preserves required extensibility.

### Current status and next step

- Draft 0.2 remains unchanged and unapproved.
- No Draft 0.3 has been written.
- No owner decisions have been made on the basis of reviewer recommendations.
- Next: provide the same independent review/consolidation prompt and the same source versions to ChatGPT, Gemini and DeepSeek. Each reviewer must verify source claims, especially `DECISIONS.md`, distinguish facts from recommendations and owner decisions, and return a traceable proposed change matrix plus prioritized owner questions.
- After comparing the three independent outputs, prepare Draft 0.3 for owner review. Obtain explicit owner approval of a numbered final specification before any implementation plan or code.
- No Rev.8 diagnostics, repair, Rev.9 code, PoC, installer, configuration or service work was performed. Production `D:\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected and untouched.


## 2026-11-08 — Detailed independent synthesis and Draft 0.3 change recommendations (Gemini + DeepSeek)

**Sources:** The complete Gemini and DeepSeek review reports pasted by the owner in this project conversation; the exact Draft 0.2, DECISIONS.md and PROJECT-LOG.md versions fetched from the public repository. **Synthesis:** ChatGPT. **Status:** Analysis added for traceability; this is not an owner decision, not Draft 0.3, and not authorization to implement.

### Source baseline and verified facts

- Review baseline: `REV9-REQUIREMENTS-DRAFT.md`, Draft 0.2, blob SHA `c71035e6f599e6c2d76becd8e762a4ebea5c7745`, commit `cee7c87d84e1dad809438cd66077b80bd9c2a918`.
- Owner decision source: `DECISIONS.md`, blob SHA `d1ee45a432133d2bff2dea8c5b5bd83e7dd85471`.
- Log before this additive entry: blob SHA `da1bf120aba59cfd568f3311974001712533fd24`.
- The current decision file does not contain an approved 10/15/20/30-second replay-duration list. DeepSeek's assertion to that effect is unsupported and must not be treated as an owner decision.
- Confirmed owner requirements remain: replay overlay independent of live/recording; continuous recording to user-selected tournament storage; compact bookmark control that does not auto-open its drawer; green Live state and “PC IP” wording; easy installation on Windows and Linux; separation of production and test environments.
- Draft 0.2 remains unapproved. No owner decision is created merely by recording a reviewer recommendation.

### Overall assessment

Both reviewers support continued specification work but do not establish an implementation-ready baseline. The consolidated assessment is **NO-GO for implementation; GO for preparing and reviewing Draft 0.3**. Rev.9 remains a clean, independent implementation from scratch; Rev.8 defects are risk references and acceptance-test inputs, not an inherited architecture.

Draft 0.2's technology-neutral approach should be preserved. Neither review authorizes a choice of SQLite/JSON, IndexedDB exclusion, MSE/rVFC, a specific API route/status code, a PIN, fixed port/interface bindings, Caddy/MediaMTX, a sidecar, a compiled supervisor, or a particular media/container pipeline.

### Corrections and disagreements between the reviews and the source

1. **Replay duration versus retrieval strategy:** The user-visible interval, available duration choices and default need an explicit product decision. However, exact-range retrieval and larger-span retrieval followed by trimming are alternative implementation strategies already permitted by Draft 0.2 Addendum B if the visible logical interval is correct. This is not, by itself, an owner decision. Do not mandate a single request or require container duration to equal the UI selection.
2. **Automatic deletion:** Gemini's suggestion to delete the oldest recording automatically at a low-space threshold is not approved and conflicts with the rule that no automatic deletion policy is adopted without explicit owner approval. Disk-full behavior must remain OPEN until the owner decides. Any disk-full test must use a disposable virtual/test volume, never the host/system disk.
3. **I/O priority:** A blanket hard write-priority rule could starve replay reads and undermine the immediate-replay requirement. Specify bounded isolation: ingest/recording must not be starved by replay, while replay must retain a measurable bounded response on the agreed reference system. The scheduling mechanism is technical.
4. **Crash recovery/media finalization:** Do not promise that a restart can always repair or “finalize the moov atom” after a crash. Require honest classification of complete, in-progress, incomplete, recoverable and unrecoverable media; identify the valid retrievable interval and never advertise a corrupt tail as complete.
5. **Two-camera synchronization:** A manual flash/clapper can be a reference event, but does not by itself prove the exact camera capture timestamp. Require measured offset, drift, mapping method and uncertainty. The owner must decide whether Rev.9 promises a numeric alignment tolerance or only reports alignment/uncertainty without claiming “synchronized” when evidence is insufficient.
6. **Frame stepping:** Exact decoded-frame stepping versus best-effort temporal stepping is a genuine capability decision. A UI label such as “approximate” is not a substitute for a working control. A prototype may establish what is feasible before the owner selects the product promise.
7. **Acceptance-test execution:** “Runnable with current requirements” means the test has no unresolved product/specification choice; it does not mean it can be executed today. All Rev.9 acceptance tests require a Rev.9 candidate build. In particular, tests must not be declared passed against Rev.8/current software. DeepSeek's suggestion that AT-051 or AT-053 can be run against the current setup is not a valid Rev.9 acceptance result. AT-043 also requires an implemented sync disclosure and a suitable measurement setup.
8. **Technology-specific browser claims:** Requirements should define observable decode, visible-presentation and frame-step behavior. Do not hard-code browser APIs such as rVFC or MSE before the media architecture and supported browsers are selected.
9. **Scope:** Single-mat/single-tournament/single-operator is a plausible initial product option, not a decision. Multi-mat, tournament and concurrent-operator scope must be explicitly asked of the owner rather than silently deferred to a later revision.
10. **Reference environment:** A named hardware/browser/network profile and numerical performance thresholds are needed before quantitative acceptance testing. They can be finalized as a Gate B/reference-profile activity if Draft 0.3 preserves the measurement model and clearly marks numeric criteria as provisional.
11. **Time zones and DST:** Event/archive time must be unambiguous across timezone and daylight-saving transitions. Use an unambiguous instant plus display-timezone context as the logical requirement; do not prescribe a database or timestamp serialization prematurely.
12. **Camera identity/reconnect:** A different physical camera must not silently inherit the identity/history of a previous camera merely because it publishes to the same logical path. Require an explicit configured logical identity and surface identity changes or uncertainty.
13. **Multi-tab/client behavior:** Silent conflicting writes or one tab unexpectedly controlling another must be prevented. Whether simultaneous operators are supported is an owner scope decision; a single-controller policy is only a candidate, not adopted.
14. **Licensing:** Compliance with third-party licenses and redistribution terms is a release requirement. The actual component/license inventory is a pre-release verification task, not an architecture decision in this review.

### Missing or under-specified requirements and recommended treatment

| Area | Draft 0.3 treatment | Decision timing |
|---|---|---|
| Replay interval semantics | Define the user-visible interval and how selected start/end are verified separately from request/container span. Keep retrieval strategy open. | Gate A for duration choices/default; technical strategy later |
| Bookmark contract | Define stable logical ID, source/event time, per-camera mapping/uncertainty, persistence guarantee, validation, duplicate/idempotent retry, concurrency and recovery. Keep storage format/database open. | Gate A logical guarantees; implementation technology later |
| Archive query outcomes | Distinguish no matching data, invalid query, unavailable archive, incomplete segment, corrupt/unreadable media and internal failure. | Specify now |
| Recording lifecycle | Define segment states, clean close, interrupted shutdown, restart discovery and the valid retrievable range. Do not promise universal crash repair. | Specify now; validate in prototype |
| Camera reconnect/identity | Define new session/boundary on reconnect and prevent silent identity substitution. | Specify now |
| Disk full/removal/slow storage | Detect destination loss and write stalls, surface recording degradation, mark affected segments incomplete, and preserve replay/live failure isolation. Do not auto-delete by default. | Detection/marking now; owner policy Gate A |
| I/O contention | Require no ingest/recording starvation and bounded replay responsiveness under concurrent load. | Specify now; numeric thresholds after reference profile |
| Endpoint security | Separate ingest, UI, replay/media, management and metrics/diagnostic exposure; specify authorization expectations without selecting binding/auth technology. | Gate A |
| Synchronization | Record offset, drift, reference method, capture-time evidence and uncertainty; distinguish aligned from approximate/unknown. | Measurement model now; tolerance Gate A |
| Health states | Each state must include evidence source, last-success/last-change time, freshness, timeout and stale/unknown handling. Keep camera-connected, frames-arriving, live-presented, recording-progress, archive-query, replay-decoded, replay-visible and storage-health separate. | Specify now |
| Logs/privacy | Require rotation/bounded growth, secret redaction and privacy-safe diagnostic export. | Core rules now; retention/size Gate B |
| Tournament/mat lifecycle | Define session close/seal/reopen and data separation; decide initial single/multi-mat, tournament and operator scope. | Scope Gate A; detailed workflow Gate B |
| Time/DST | Define unambiguous event instants and display timezone; test repeated/skipped local times. | Logical rule now; display policy Gate B |
| Browser tabs/clients | Prevent silent concurrent-write/control conflicts; specify visible conflict/error handling. | Core rule now; concurrent operator scope Gate A |
| Display/touch/accessibility | Require primary controls to be usable on the supported display/input classes; do not invent pixel values without device profile. | Basic requirement now; detailed targets Gate B |
| Windows/Linux/lifecycle | Preserve confirmed easy-install requirement on both platforms; define supported versions/browser matrix and start/stop/update/rollback expectations. | Support matrix Gate A; installer mechanism later |
| Export/backup | State whether bookmark/log export/import and recording backup are required for initial release; if deferred, document recovery/portability limits. | Gate B unless owner requires it at launch |
| Licensing | Require license/redistribution review before packaging/release. | Before release |

### Acceptance-test classification — preliminary mapping

The categories below classify specification readiness, not present-day execution. **A** = sufficiently specified to execute against a future Rev.9 build without a further Gate A product decision. **B** = blocked or materially affected by an owner decision. **C** = needs a prototype, reference hardware/browser, multi-camera rig, disposable storage or other controlled test environment. A test may appear in both B and C.

- **A — requirements sufficiently defined:** AT-001, AT-002, AT-003, AT-012, AT-015, AT-016, AT-020, AT-021, AT-030, AT-031, AT-042, AT-053, AT-054, AT-060, AT-074. This is a preliminary classification; confirm the exact pass criteria in Draft 0.2 before marking any test fully unblocked. AT-053 must be run on Rev.9, not Rev.8.
- **B — owner decision required:** AT-004 (return-to-live target), AT-010 (duration choices/default and visible interval), AT-011 (search horizon), AT-013 (seek accuracy), AT-014 (frame-step promise), AT-032 and AT-033 (bookmark post-roll/availability), AT-034 and AT-035 (client concurrency and interrupted/idempotent writes), AT-040 and AT-041 (sync criteria), AT-043 (what “aligned” may be claimed), AT-050 (ingest exposure), AT-051 (management exposure), AT-052 (authorization), AT-061 (low-space threshold), AT-062 (disk-full policy), AT-063 (retention/deletion), AT-070 and AT-071 (supported platform/installation matrix), AT-072, AT-073, AT-075 and AT-076 (installation/update lifecycle), AT-080 (duration contract), AT-082 (archive horizon/search semantics), AT-085 (bookmark time mapping convention), AT-087 (bookmark API/concurrency contract), AT-090 (frame-step truthfulness).
- **C — prototype/reference environment or controlled rig required:** AT-005 (failure injection/isolation), AT-022 (interrupted shutdown/recovery), AT-023 (runtime/media/file naming semantics), AT-024 (long-run/reference hardware), AT-040 and AT-041 (repeatable synchronization rig), AT-042 (multi-camera rig where applicable), AT-043 (implemented sync disclosure), AT-061 and AT-062 (disposable virtual volume), AT-064 (storage/recovery test setup), AT-070 and AT-071 (clean OS virtual machines), AT-081 (proxy/HLS or selected transport regression on the Rev.9 architecture), AT-083 (decoded-frame and visible-presentation evidence), AT-084 (end-to-end timing instrumentation), AT-086 (health evidence/freshness implementation), AT-088 (failure-containment prototype), AT-089 (sync measurement rig), AT-090 (frame-step prototype).
- **Not runnable today:** None of the above should be reported as a passed Rev.9 test until a Rev.9 candidate exists. Tests marked A are only specification-ready. The test IDs and their exact titles/pass conditions remain governed by Draft 0.2; Draft 0.3 should add a test-by-test matrix with prerequisites, evidence, safe setup and status (not run/pass/fail/blocked).

### Gate A / Gate B / deferred owner decisions

**Gate A — resolve before approving an implementation baseline:**
1. User-visible replay interval semantics, initial duration choices and default. This does not choose the underlying retrieval strategy.
2. Frame-step capability promised to the operator (exact decoded frame or best-effort temporal step).
3. Bookmark logical contract and persistence guarantee, including event-time mapping, duplicate/retry behavior and the required pre/post-roll policy or configurability.
4. Disk-full, destination-removal and write-stall policy, including explicit deletion boundary. Automatic deletion remains unapproved unless the owner expressly opts in.
5. Exposure and authorization policy for ingest, UI, replay/media, management and metrics.
6. Two-camera synchronization promise: numeric tolerance or an honest uncertainty-only/approximate status; supported camera/capture conditions.
7. Initial supported Windows/Linux versions, browser and camera matrix sufficient to define product support.
8. Initial scope: number of mats, tournaments and simultaneous operators. Do not assume single-mat/single-operator.
9. Any numeric performance promise that the UI or acceptance tests will claim. If values are not yet chosen, explicitly keep them non-binding and require a Gate B reference-profile decision before quantitative acceptance.

**Gate B — decide before quantitative validation/release, but not necessarily before the logical spec can be approved:**
- Reference PC/CPU/GPU/RAM/storage, display, browser versions, network topology and camera configuration.
- Numeric replay-open, seek, return-live, recording-stall and low-space thresholds with measurement procedure.
- Log retention/size limits, export format, bookmark export/import and backup policy.
- Detailed touch/display dimensions, contrast and accessibility targets.
- Detailed multi-tournament/multi-mat workflow if initial scope is narrower.
- Installer packaging, update/rollback mechanism, launcher versus supervisor, storage/database/media technologies and preload/cache strategy.
- Third-party component/license inventory and redistribution review before release.

**Deferred only by explicit scope statement:** Audio behavior, advanced zoom limits, multi-operator collaboration, export/import, and other secondary features may be deferred if their absence is clear and does not compromise the core replay/recording/safety requirements. Deferral must not silently remove data portability, privacy, recoverability or future extensibility needs.

### Recommended Draft 0.3 change set

1. Add a source/version traceability table for Draft 0.2, DECISIONS.md and the historical FR-022/FR-023 reference; retain the correction that no 10/15/20/30-second owner decision exists.
2. Clarify in the replay section the user-visible logical interval, selectable values/default as OPEN, and the distinction between requested interval, API span, media/container interval and visible frames. Preserve exact-range and larger-span-plus-trim as technical alternatives.
3. Add a Gate A/Gate B/deferred register with owner decision status and rationale.
4. Add logical bookmark identity, timestamp mapping, persistence, idempotency, concurrency, validation, error and recovery contracts without prescribing database/file format.
5. Add explicit archive query error/outcome taxonomy and segment complete/in-progress/incomplete/corrupt states.
6. Add application lifecycle and interrupted-shutdown/restart requirements; do not promise repair of every interrupted media file.
7. Add camera identity, reconnect and new-session boundary requirements.
8. Add storage removal, slow-write, disk-full and I/O contention requirements; prohibit destructive test execution on host/system volumes.
9. Add endpoint exposure/authorization matrix while leaving binding addresses, ports and auth mechanism open.
10. Add synchronization evidence, offset/drift/uncertainty and aligned/approximate/unknown states; keep numeric tolerance open for owner decision.
11. Add health evidence/freshness/timeout and stale/unknown state rules.
12. Add log rotation, privacy redaction and bounded-growth requirements; keep exact retention period open.
13. Add multi-tab/client and tournament/mat scope questions, plus explicit session lifecycle.
14. Add unambiguous event-time/timezone/DST semantics.
15. Add Windows/Linux/browser/camera support matrix and app lifecycle expectations while preserving the already-confirmed Windows/Linux easy-install requirement.
16. Add display/touch usability requirements without unsupported pixel thresholds.
17. Add licensing/redistribution compliance as a pre-release requirement.
18. Rework the acceptance-test appendix into a row-per-test matrix: test ID/title, requirement trace, A/B/C status, owner decision dependency, prototype/environment, safe procedure, evidence, pass/fail criteria and status. Add new tests for removable storage, write stalls, DST, concurrent tabs/clients, camera identity/reconnect, app crash/restart and log/privacy behavior as appropriate.
19. Keep all technology/architecture proposals explicitly non-normative until selected through a separate architecture evaluation.

### Decision and safety record

- This entry records recommendations and analysis only. It does not alter Draft 0.2 or DECISIONS.md and does not create owner decisions.
- Draft 0.3 has **not** been created by this entry.

## 2026-11-08 — Owner confirms free-tool compatibility and no paid dependencies

**Source:** Explicit owner instruction in the JudoReplayKit project conversation. **Status:** Confirmed product requirements; technical implementation remains open.

The owner clarified that the final Rev.9 specification must ensure:
- Easy installation on both Windows and Linux.
- Compatibility with free streaming tools such as PRISM Live Studio.
- No mandatory payment anywhere for JudoReplayKit to provide its intended core functionality optimally. No required subscription, paid cloud service, paid account, paid plugin, paid runtime, or paid third-party license.
- Essential functionality (live ingest, continuous recording, archive/replay, bookmarks, supported multi-camera operation and local use) must not be gated behind a paid tier.
- Prefer open/documented protocols and interoperability rather than a dependency on one named streaming application.
- Third-party paid extras may exist in optional external tools, but JudoReplayKit must not require them. PRISM Plus or other premium tiers must not be required for the integration.
- Verify license and redistribution rights for every bundled component before release.

### Compatibility facts checked for specification planning

- PRISM's official desktop download page currently lists Windows and macOS, not Linux. Its official FAQ documents Custom RTMP and additional streaming protocol support. PRISM's subscription announcement states that core live streaming remains free while selected premium features are subscription-only. Therefore, PRISM is a compatibility target on platforms where PRISM itself is supported, using free functionality; it is not a Linux client requirement. Sources: https://prismlive.com/en_us/desktop.html ; https://prismlive.com/en_us/faq/faq.html ; https://guide.prismlive.com/desktop/announcement/general/upcoming-subscription-model-for-prism-live-studio
- OBS Studio's official site describes it as free/open-source and offers Windows, macOS and Linux downloads. OBS is a suitable cross-platform compatibility target, subject to protocol/version testing. Sources: https://obsproject.com/ ; https://obsproject.com/download

### Consequences for the final review and acceptance tests

1. Add a normative “No mandatory paid dependencies” requirement and a dependency/license inventory.
2. Define a protocol-neutral integration contract, then select a small documented protocol matrix that can be implemented and tested without paid services. Do not hard-code PRISM-specific behavior into the core.
3. Add acceptance tests for PRISM Custom RTMP on a supported PRISM platform and OBS on Windows and Linux, using free features only. Include reconnect, stream loss, sustained ingest, recording continuity and two-camera behavior where supported.
4. Verify that the complete core workflow works locally without account registration, paid cloud relay, subscription validation or internet access after installation.
5. Make Windows and Linux installation and upgrade/uninstall paths independently testable, with no manual editing of configuration files for ordinary users.
6. Keep external destination costs (for example, a user-chosen public streaming platform) separate from JudoReplayKit operation. JudoReplayKit itself must not require a paid destination to ingest, record and replay locally.

This entry adds owner requirements to DECISIONS.md. It does not select RTMP/SRT/another protocol as the final architecture, does not promise PRISM availability on Linux, and does not authorize implementation before the final numbered specification is approved.


## 2026-11-08 — Owner answers to Rev.9 scope questions

The owner clarified and confirmed the following for the final Rev.9 specification:

- **Replay look-back:** Accepted 5/10/15/20/30-second choices with 15 seconds default, after clarification that this is the interval immediately preceding the Replay button press and ending at that press time. Example: click at 14:32:10 with 15 seconds selected means 14:31:55–14:32:10. This is the logical user-visible interval; the media/API request may use a different internal span but must present the selected interval accurately.
- **Frame-by-frame:** Owner selects exact frame stepping (one actual decoded frame forward/backward), not approximate temporal jumps.
- **Bookmarks:** Must be creatable from inside Instant Replay. Bookmark time must refer to the event/media position being reviewed. Retain 30 seconds pre-roll and 15 seconds post-roll. The specification must describe how post-roll is acquired and when the bookmark becomes fully available.
- **Recording retention and deletion:** Owner selects no automatic deletion (option C). User manages retention. Any deletion requires explicit approval before files are removed. Low-storage warnings and clear deletion scope are required; automatic cleanup is not authorized.
- **Two-camera synchronization:** Automatic alignment with an honest indication of measured offset/uncertainty; no unvalidated numeric guarantee.
- **Network access:** Owner selects local access with optional password protection (option C), provided the password option does not undermine ease of use. The final design must balance simple local operation with safe exposure of management/replay endpoints.
- **Tournament/mat scope:** Still open. The earlier “one tournament with multiple mats” question was not understood. Before specification freeze, explain with a concrete example whether the software should manage one match/camera pair at a time or several simultaneous mats, each with its own live feeds, recording and replay, under one tournament.
- **PRISM:** PRISM is to be used on the mobile device or camera that streams video. The owner is not asking to run PRISM on the PC/Linux host. Therefore test the incoming stream from the mobile/camera using PRISM's free supported streaming capability; do not treat desktop PRISM as a JudoReplayKit dependency. OBS can remain a free PC/Linux interoperability/test source where useful. No paid tier or service may be required.
- **Performance:** Owner agrees to define functional immediacy/independence/return-to-live requirements now and establish exact numeric timing thresholds only after practical tests on a defined reference setup.

No implementation or architecture choice is implied by these answers. The final spec must preserve easy Windows/Linux installation, no mandatory paid dependencies, and protection of all existing production/test installations and recordings. No code or runtime was changed as part of this decision update.


## 2026-11-08 — Owner clarifies bookmark semantics, multi-mat use and equipment

The owner clarified three important product points:

### Bookmarks are markers, not clips
A bookmark is only a marker that leads the user to a moment in the continuous recording. Its typical use is post-tournament learning/review. There is no requirement to generate a fixed clip or automatically retain 30 seconds before and 15 seconds after every bookmark; that earlier proposal is withdrawn. The user can inspect the surrounding full recording as needed. Bookmark creation must remain possible from Instant Replay, and the marker must point to the media/event position being viewed, not simply the time the button was clicked. The owner has not said that live-time bookmarking should be removed. The continuous archive is the source of truth for later retrieval.

### Multi-mat and supervisor workflow
The owner considers multi-mat support a good idea if a supervisor can switch between all mats to inspect their live feeds and Instant Replay. However, the priority is the individual mat referee's workflow: see the live camera feed with a selected delay and use Instant Replay quickly. Multi-mat/supervisor functionality must be additional and must not compromise the speed, simplicity or independence of each mat station. Treat per-mat operation as the baseline and supervisor cross-mat switching as a desired capability to assess; maximum mat count and first-release scope are still not fixed.

### Expected equipment for each mat
The owner's planned kit per mat is:
1. One PC running Windows or Linux; ChromeOS is desirable if it is not disproportionately difficult to support.
2. One router.
3. Two mobile phones or other streaming cameras, such as GoPro-class devices.
4. A power strip and/or extension cable reel with outlets.
5. One wireless mouse.

Windows and Linux remain the priority supported PC platforms. ChromeOS is conditional/exploratory and must not be promised until feasibility is assessed. The specification and test plan should account for two simultaneous camera sources per mat and the local router-based network. Support for specific phone/action-camera models must be validated by protocol and device testing, not assumed from brand name alone.

No code, installation, runtime or recording data was changed. These clarifications update the product requirements only; implementation remains gated on approval of the final Rev.9 specification.


## 2026-11-08 — Owner makes supervisor function optional and adds camera tripods

**Source:** Explicit owner clarification in the project conversation. **Status:** Confirmed product scope/equipment clarification.

- The supervisor function (switching between mats to view their live feeds and Instant Replay) is **nice-to-have only**. It must not complicate the project or compromise the primary workflow for the referee at each individual mat. It may be deferred or omitted from the initial release if it adds material complexity. The baseline remains simple, independent per-mat operation.
- Add **two camera tripods per mat** to the expected equipment.
- The two cameras are positioned in **opposite corners of the competition area** so the techniques performed by the athletes can be viewed from two different angles.
- Requirements and practical testing should consider two simultaneous camera feeds, stable tripod mounting and complementary viewpoints. Exact camera height, lens, framing and placement geometry are not yet specified and should be validated during setup tests.

No code, installation, services or recordings were changed. This is a requirements clarification only; Rev.9 implementation remains gated on approval of the final numbered specification.


## 2026-11-08 — Rev.9 Requirements Draft 0.3 prepared for final review

**Status:** Requirements document updated; no implementation approved or performed. Draft 0.3 supersedes Draft 0.2 as the current review candidate. Draft 0.2 remains in Git history.

### Owner decisions consolidated into Draft 0.3
- Per-mat baseline: one Windows/Linux PC, one router, two simultaneous streaming cameras, two tripods, power strip/extension cable reel and wireless mouse.
- Cameras are positioned in opposite corners of the competition area to provide two different viewing angles.
- Supervisor/cross-mat switching is strictly nice-to-have. It may be omitted from the initial release and must not complicate, delay or degrade the referee's per-mat workflow.
- Instant Replay look-back choices: 5/10/15/20/30 seconds; 15 seconds default; the visible interval ends at the Replay press time.
- Exact frame stepping is required: one actual decoded frame forward/backward; implementation feasibility must be demonstrated and no approximate seek may be mislabeled as exact.
- Bookmarks are markers into the continuous archive, not clips. No fixed pre-roll/post-roll. The old 30-second/15-second proposal is explicitly withdrawn. A bookmark from Instant Replay maps to the media position being viewed.
- No automatic deletion; any deletion requires explicit approval of exact scope.
- Mat referee needs selectable-delay live viewing in addition to Instant Replay; the delay choices/default remain open.
- Two-camera alignment is desired with honest measured offset/uncertainty, without unsupported numeric guarantee.
- Windows/Linux easy installation, local offline core operation and no mandatory paid dependencies remain requirements. PRISM is a free mobile/camera source where supported; OBS is a free Windows/Linux interoperability target. ChromeOS remains conditional.

### Draft 0.3 contents and review focus
Draft 0.3 updates the requirements, invariants, per-mat deployment, replay semantics, frame stepping, bookmark contract, storage safety, sync, local security, installation/licensing, test plan, Gate A/B register and explicit non-goals. It deliberately leaves protocol, database, media server, process topology, installer technology and unapproved numeric thresholds open.

Reviewers should particularly verify:
1. No contradiction between owner decisions and the normative requirements.
2. Exact logical replay interval and media/request span distinction.
3. Exact decoded-frame stepping feasibility and honest UI claims.
4. Marker-only bookmarks and event/media-time mapping.
5. Two-camera/two-tripod opposite-corner baseline and useful practical camera test.
6. No automatic deletion and safe storage-full behavior.
7. Local-first/no-paid-dependency requirements and PRISM/OBS platform wording.
8. Supervisor optionality and no complexity creep into the per-mat workflow.
9. Missing edge cases, security risks, acceptance-test completeness and unresolved Gate A items.

### Review procedure
- Send the exact same Draft 0.3 text to Gemini and DeepSeek.
- Give both reviewers the same prompt and require findings to be classified as blocker, important, minor or recommendation.
- Reviewers must not convert their own technology preferences into owner decisions, and must distinguish facts, risks, assumptions and recommendations.
- Resolve only real contradictions, safety gaps or material testability issues. Do not create an endless review loop.
- After the one review round, present the resulting numbered final specification to the owner for explicit approval. No Rev.9 code, installer, service or production/test environment changes before that approval.

### Safety and scope record
No Rev.8 or production files, services, recordings or installations were touched in preparing Draft 0.3. No code was written, no service was started/stopped, and no acceptance test was claimed as passed. Production `D:\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected.

## 2026-11-08 — Owner resolves final Draft 0.3 review questions

**Source:** Explicit owner answers in the project conversation. **Status:** Confirmed product requirements. These answers close the six outstanding questions raised after the Gemini and DeepSeek reviews.

1. **Replay at live edge — YES:** Open immediately; do not wait for segment finalization. If the full selected interval is not yet available, show the footage/interval actually available and clearly disclose the limitation.
2. **Two-camera bookmarks — YES:** One bookmark represents one shared event/reference moment. Store the shared event time and each camera's mapped media position. Opening the bookmark should show both views wherever their recordings cover the event; perfect frame/time identity between views is not required.
3. **Frame-by-frame — YES, with clarification:** The operator can select either camera and inspect that camera independently. Each step is exactly one actual decoded frame forward/backward in the selected camera. The two cameras do not need to show identical frames at identical times.
4. **Live Delay vs Instant Replay — YES:** Independent controls. Replay look-back ends at the instant Replay is pressed and is not shifted by the selected Live Delay.
5. **Storage full — YES:** Warn and stop recording in a controlled manner before the destination is exhausted. Preserve existing recordings; never auto-delete. Any deletion requires explicit approval of the exact scope. Safety reserve is established by testing, not an assumed fixed value.
6. **Live Delay — CONFIRMED:** Choices 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 seconds; default 2 seconds.

**Additional new owner requirement:** Instant Replay must support slow-motion playback. Exact speed choices and audio behavior remain open for feasibility/practical validation. Slow motion must remain independent of live viewing and continuous recording.

### Consequent Draft 0.4 changes
- Replace earlier OPEN live-delay choices/default with 1–5 seconds and 2-second default.
- Define immediate live-edge replay and truthful partial-availability behavior.
- Define shared bookmark event/reference plus per-camera mapped media positions.
- Clarify independent exact frame stepping in either selected camera; no requirement that both cameras step in identical temporal lockstep.
- Add slow-motion playback as a confirmed requirement and a dedicated acceptance test; leave exact speed values/audio behavior open.
- Clarify controlled pre-exhaustion recording stop and test-derived reserve; no fixed 500 MB assumption and no deletion.
- Add/strengthen acceptance tests for live-edge replay, dual-camera bookmarks, per-camera exact stepping and slow motion.
- Remove resolved live delay and disk-full policy questions from Gate A.

**Review/scope:** This is the final correction round after the single Gemini + DeepSeek review of Draft 0.3. Draft 0.4 is the corrected owner-approval candidate; it is not approved until the owner explicitly approves it. No implementation, code, installer, service, recording or protected environment was changed.

## 2026-11-08 — Rev.9 isolated development location and AI review workflow

### Approved AI-assisted workflow

**Owner decision (2026-11-08):** Proceed with the proposed staged workflow, using the available AI assistants as independent reviewers rather than parallel implementation teams.

1. **Gemini Pro — first architecture proposal.** Give Gemini the approved Rev.9 requirements and decisions. Ask for 2–3 realistic architecture alternatives, explicit trade-offs, technical feasibility analysis and the smallest proof-of-concept. Do not ask it to write the full application yet.

Ask it to challenge assumptions, identify overlooked failure modes and independently assess the critical requirements. 4. **ChatGPT — synthesis and project quality control.** Compare all assessments against the approved requirements, preserve disagreements and source attribution, identify what needs a prototype, and present a consolidated recommendation to the owner before any architecture is locked or implementation begins.

The initial architecture/prototype work must prioritize evidence for:
- Immediate live-edge replay while continuous recording is still in progress, without waiting for segment finalization.

- Replay and slow motion isolated from live presentation, Live Delay, ingest and recording.

The smallest proof-of-concept should test the highest-risk media assumptions before investing in the full UI or application. Any test recording/data must be disposable and explicitly designated for testing. No existing tournament recordings or protected installation data may be used for destructive tests.

## 2026-11-08 — Gemini Pro: initial Rev.9 architecture/specification review (owner-supplied response)

**Source:** Complete response pasted by the owner in the JudoReplayKit project conversation.  
**Attribution:** Gemini Pro.  
**Status:** Advisory review recorded; not an owner decision, not an approved specification change, and not an architecture selection.

### Important document/version discrepancy

Gemini's response says it reviewed **Draft 0.3** and recommends corrections to Draft 0.3. The task given to Gemini linked the current **Draft 0.4**, which the owner had already approved on 2026-11-08. Its references to Draft 0.3 and its request for another final owner approval therefore do not establish that the approved Draft 0.4 is deficient. Before relying on any finding, reviewers must identify the exact document version and relevant section they actually inspected.

### Gemini's findings, preserved as attributed opinions

**Overall verdict:** Gemini labelled the specification “CONDITIONAL GO” and considered it detailed about system invariants and failure containment, while raising concerns about container finalization, delayed-live synchronization, time mapping, exact frame stepping, I/O contention, multi-mat scope, timezone handling and browser fMP4 playback.

**Blocking findings raised by Gemini:**
- **B-1 — Safe stop/container finalization:** Gemini argues that a recording engine must cleanly finalize/close open recording segments before stopping at the storage safety threshold, to preserve already captured footage. It specifically discusses fMP4 and the possibility of an incomplete/unreadable trailing segment if the filesystem becomes exhausted.
- **B-2 — Synchronization of delayed live views:** Gemini asks whether both delayed live feeds must be synchronized to each other, not merely delayed individually.
- **B-3 — Time mapping without source capture timecodes:** Gemini notes that mobile RTMP sources may not supply absolute capture timestamps and asks for an explicit fallback mapping based on server receipt time and buffer offsets.

**Important findings raised:**
- **I-1 — Exact backward frame stepping:** Gemini highlights the difficulty of reverse stepping in inter-frame codecs and the potential cost of decoding forward from a prior keyframe. It recommends prototype evidence rather than assuming ordinary HTML5 video seeking is frame-exact.
- **I-2 — Read/write contention:** Gemini recommends protecting continuous ingest/recording from replay-read contention. Its proposed strict write-priority wording is a suggestion, not an approved requirement or selected scheduling design.

**Minor findings raised:**
- **M-1 — Multi-mat scope:** Gemini recommends explicitly excluding supervisor/multi-mat routing and UI until the single-mat baseline passes acceptance tests.
- **M-2 — Time representation:** Gemini recommends monotonic time or UTC for internal timelines, with local timezone conversion only for UI display.

**Additional technical risks/opinions:**
- Browser fMP4 replay may require correct initialization/media-fragment handling (Gemini mentions `moov`, byte-range behavior and possible MSE/remux approaches). This is a technical hypothesis to investigate against the selected implementation, not a diagnosis of Rev.9 or proof that fMP4 must be used.
- Gemini considers tight synchronization of two independent Wi-Fi mobile streams difficult without genlock and warns against promising perfect synchronization.
- Gemini proposes tests AT-045 (safe stop/storage reserve) and AT-017a (delayed-live synchronization), and proposes strengthening a duration test across multiple interval choices. These are suggestions for checking against the existing Draft 0.4 test plan, not approved new test IDs.

### Independent cross-check against already approved Draft 0.4

The following points are already substantially covered or resolved in Draft 0.4 and must not be treated as missing without identifying a precise remaining gap:
- **Storage safety:** Draft 0.4 requires a controlled stop before storage exhaustion, preservation of existing recordings, honest classification of an incomplete tail segment, and a test-derived safety reserve. It does not mandate fMP4. A cleanly closed/finalized segment is a reasonable implementation objective, but the implementation must not assume a particular container or promise that data still in volatile buffers can be recovered after hard storage failure.
- **Delayed-live camera alignment:** Draft 0.4 already includes confirmed automatic alignment using measured per-camera timing and honest offset/uncertainty. Acceptance test **AT-017** explicitly covers dual-camera live-delay alignment. Gemini's proposed AT-017a appears duplicative unless it identifies a distinct uncovered behavior.
- **Capture-time fallback:** Draft 0.4 already cautions against claiming capture-time synchronization when only ingest-time evidence exists. Its timing model and uncertainty requirements require an honest mapping. A concrete fallback algorithm should be designed and tested during architecture/prototype work; it should not be prematurely hardcoded into the requirements as “ingest time minus buffer” without validating what the source and pipeline actually expose.
- **Exact frame stepping:** Draft 0.4 explicitly requires one actual decoded frame per action, independently for either selected camera, and includes AT-023 and AT-092 plus a feasibility gate. Gemini's warning reinforces the need for the planned proof-of-concept; it does not justify downgrading the requirement to approximate seeking.
- **Multi-mat:** Draft 0.4 already says supervisor/multi-mat is optional and may be omitted from initial release. Deferring it until single-mat acceptance is consistent with the approved scope, but no new requirement is necessary unless implementation planning reveals scope creep.
- **Time model:** The approved spec already requires timestamp mapping and honest uncertainty, but a single explicit internal timebase policy (monotonic clock for durations/order, UTC or equivalent normalized epoch for persisted cross-process/event timestamps, local timezone only for display) remains a useful architecture-level design item to evaluate. Do not conflate wall-clock time with monotonic elapsed time.
- **Live Delay values:** Gemini's response incorrectly lists live-delay choices/default as an owner decision still required. Draft 0.4 already confirms **1, 2, 3, 4, 5 seconds**, with **2 seconds default**.
- **Architecture/platform choices:** Authentication defaults, exact supported OS/browser matrix and implementation technologies remain open, as Draft 0.4 states. These are not permission to change the confirmed functional requirements.

### Review disposition / next workflow step

- Retain Gemini's useful technical risks as review input, especially safe segment closure, exact reverse frame stepping, source timestamp limitations, delayed-view alignment and read/write contention.
- Do not apply Gemini's proposed Draft 0.3 corrections to the already approved Draft 0.4 wholesale.
- The next planned step is the independent **DeepSeek critical review**. Give DeepSeek both the authoritative Draft 0.4 and Gemini's complete response, and explicitly ask it to verify each Gemini claim against Draft 0.4, identify any new risks, and distinguish confirmed gaps from items already covered.
- Mistral may then perform an additional implementation/dependency/licensing review.
- No code, architecture, requirement or local file was changed as a result of this Gemini review. Protected Rev.8/production environments remain untouched.


## 2026-11-15 — Rev.9 consolidated AI architecture-review checkpoint

**Source:** Owner supplied the original Gemini and DeepSeek reviews and the subsequent Mistral Rev.9 technical review in the project conversation. This entry records a preliminary cross-review synthesis by ChatGPT. It is an attributed review record, not an owner decision and not a claim that any proposed architecture or PoC has been technically validated.

### Scope and baseline

- The authoritative product baseline remains **REV.9 REQUIREMENTS DRAFT 0.4**, approved by the owner on 2026-11-08.
- The Decisions document and this Project Log remain supplementary records.
- No product requirement is changed, weakened or reopened by this review.
- No architecture, programming language, media server, codec/container, protocol, database, browser pipeline, installer or dependency is approved by this entry.
- The older Repair 1 rev.4, Stage 1 and Rev.5 Fix A–F workstreams are out of scope.

### Comparative position of the three reviews

| Topic | Gemini | DeepSeek | Mistral | Preliminary synthesis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Overall direction | Presented A/B/C and strongly favoured native architecture B, subject to PoCs | Conditional GO for isolated PoCs; introduced/refined hybrid architecture D as promising | Conditional GO for isolated PoCs; favoured D as the strongest candidate, not approved | There is convergence on PoC-first and no-go for full implementation. D is a candidate, not a decision. |
| Immediate replay during active recording | Considered feasible with MediaMTX/fMP4 and native growing-file playback, but proof needed | Flagged active fMP4 parts, finalization and live-edge handling as high risk | Considered structurally possible, but requires own implementation and proof; prior Rev.8 observations are not a Rev.9 proof | Highest-priority feasibility test. Reading a growing file or listing a recording is not equivalent to fast, reliable user-visible replay. |
| Exact decoded-frame stepping | Identified reverse stepping as difficult; initially treated native player capability too optimistically | Warned libmpv alone does not guarantee exact reverse stepping; proposed explicit frame cache | Also challenged libmpv frame-back-step guarantees; favoured explicit cache | Strong agreement that ordinary player controls cannot be assumed to meet the requirement. Must prove adjacent decoded-frame identity in both directions, including after seek. |
| Browser/WebCodecs | Proposed advanced web architecture A using MSE/WebCodecs | Retained WebCodecs as a possible route but unproven | Highlighted WebCodecs secure-context constraints for LAN-IP HTTP access | Keep A in comparison until a targeted localhost-versus-LAN-origin test establishes the actual supported access path and fallback. |
| Two-camera timing | Discussed ingest timestamps and alignment limitations | Emphasized ingest arrival is not capture time; recommended shared-event tests and uncertainty reporting | Emphasized measured timing and honest uncertainty | All agree that timing evidence and capture-time claims must be distinguished. Use repeated shared visual events to measure offset and drift. |
| Replay/recording isolation | Raised read/write contention and priority concerns | Warned that OS scheduling alone cannot guarantee isolation | Noted ionice is not available on Windows; recommended architectural isolation | Isolation must be demonstrated under simultaneous dual-camera ingest, recording, replay and slow motion on Windows and Linux. |
| Storage safety | Discussed controlled close/finalization and reserve | Challenged fixed reserve assumptions and automatic recovery claims | Highlighted MediaMTX recordDeleteAfter and the need to disable automatic deletion | Draft 0.4 remains authoritative: no auto-delete; test-derived reserve; controlled stop; preserve prior recordings. Verify actual defaults/configuration and recovery behavior. |
| Licensing/platform | Raised FFmpeg licensing/build configuration | Raised Qt/PySide/PyQt and FFmpeg distribution obligations | Added browser codec/HEVC and Windows/Linux distribution concerns | Perform a concrete dependency/build/redistribution audit before selecting components. No generic “free” label is sufficient evidence. |
| Architecture candidate | B (native) | D (hybrid/native with explicit frame cache) | D (hybrid/native with explicit frame cache) | B and D may overlap: treat D as a refinement/pattern within a native/hybrid design, not necessarily a wholly separate architecture. A remains an alternative; C remains lower-priority but not disproven. |

### Consolidated technical risk register

1. **Live-edge replay while recording is active — critical / unproven.** A recording list, a growing file, or an HTTP 200 response does not prove that the operator can immediately decode and display the requested interval while the writer continues. Test the real user-visible path, both cameras, all look-back choices, segment/part boundaries and continued recording.
2. **Exact backward and forward frame stepping — critical / unproven.** A player API name or successful seek is not proof of exactly one adjacent decoded frame per action. Use visible unique frame identities and test both directions, repeated actions, seek/pause/resume, GOP variations and variable frame rate where supported.
3. **Two-camera alignment and drift — high / unproven.** Establish what timestamps each source and pipeline exposes. Measure initial offset, drift and uncertainty using repeated shared visual events. Do not label ingest-arrival time as capture time.
4. **Isolation under load — high / unproven.** Verify that replay, frame-cache work and slow motion do not interrupt camera ingest, continuous recording, live presentation or Live Delay.
5. **Storage exhaustion and segment integrity — high / unproven.** Test on disposable storage only. Establish a measured safety reserve and prove controlled stop/preservation without deletion.
6. **LAN browser security/codec matrix — high / unproven.** Test the actual intended Windows/Linux browsers and origins, including localhost and LAN-IP access. Confirm codec availability without requiring paid components.
7. **Dependency and redistribution licensing — high / unproven.** Audit exact binaries, build flags, linked libraries, codecs, Qt bindings and distribution obligations for the chosen package.

### Preliminary architecture disposition

- **A — Advanced web/MSE/WebCodecs:** retain as a candidate pending a focused secure-context, codec and exact-frame prototype.
- **B — Native media playback/framework:** retain as a candidate, but do not assume libmpv alone satisfies exact backward stepping or active-growing-recording playback.
- **C — Backend frame extraction/delivery:** lower-priority candidate due likely CPU, bandwidth and latency costs; not formally rejected without comparative measurement.
- **D — Native/hybrid with explicit decoded-frame cache:** currently the strongest working hypothesis for exact frame control, but it introduces implementation and maintenance responsibility and has not been proven. Do not lock it.

### Recommended PoC order

1. **PoC 1 — Live-edge replay while recording continues.** Measure button-to-first-usable-frame latency; test both cameras, all required look-back intervals, active parts/segment boundaries, partial availability and uninterrupted recording.
2. **PoC 2 — Exact decoded-frame stepping.** Compare the viable browser and native approaches on identical numbered footage; prove exactly one adjacent frame per action in both directions, including after seek and with representative GOP/frame-rate conditions.
3. **PoC 3 — Dual-camera timing.** Repeated shared visual events at start and over a sustained run; report offset, drift and uncertainty and identify timestamp provenance.
4. **PoC 4 — Isolation/performance.** Simultaneous dual ingest/recording, live display, Live Delay, replay and slow motion; measure continuity, latency, resource use and dropped frames.
5. **PoC 5 — Storage safety.** Disposable test volume only; low-space warning, controlled stop, interrupted process and recovery; confirm no deletion and preservation of completed recordings.

Pass/fail thresholds not already defined by Draft 0.4 must be established from baseline measurements and approved by the owner before being treated as acceptance criteria. No arbitrary numeric thresholds are adopted here.

### Next independent review round

The owner intends to give Gemini and DeepSeek the same consolidated comparison brief and request that each independently:
- Verify or challenge the comparison above against the full original source materials.
- Identify any misrepresentation, omitted claim, unsupported conclusion or disagreement.
- Evaluate A–D using the same requirements and PoC order.
- State which conclusions are evidence-backed versus hypotheses.
- Return corrections and additions without changing Draft 0.4 or selecting an architecture.

The two reviewers must receive the same prompt and the same five source materials (Draft 0.4, Decisions, Project Log, Gemini original report, DeepSeek original report), plus the Mistral report and this comparison. Their responses are advisory and will be reconciled before owner approval.

### Status and safety

- **Current verdict:** CONDITIONAL GO for isolated PoCs only; NO-GO for architecture lock and full implementation until critical feasibility evidence exists.
- This is a technical review status, not approval to weaken or alter any requirement.
- No application code, installer, service, configuration or local environment was changed by this review.
- Production `D:\\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected.
- Rev.9 work remains isolated in `C:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev9_Dev`; this entry does not claim that local workspace was inspected.

## 2026-11-15 — DeepSeek cross-review and consolidated PoC refinement

**Source:** Full DeepSeek cross-review supplied by the owner in the JudoReplayKit project conversation (480-line report). **Attribution:** DeepSeek. **Independent assessment:** ChatGPT. **Status:** Advisory technical review only; no requirements, architecture or implementation decision is changed by this entry.

### DeepSeek's stated review scope and limitations

DeepSeek describes this as a cross-review of the Gemini, Mistral and its own earlier technical reviews. It reports that it received the Requirements Draft 0.4, DECISIONS.md and PROJECT-LOG.md as text, but did not independently verify their hashes; it did not run tests; and it did not independently verify every technical source. It also notes that the original Gemini proposal was not supplied as a separate file in that review context. Accordingly, its findings are recorded as a review, not as test evidence.

DeepSeek's overall verdict is **CONDITIONAL GO for isolated PoCs only** and **NO-GO for architecture lock or full implementation** until the critical feasibility work and relevant owner gates are complete.

### DeepSeek's main cross-review conclusions

- It reports broad agreement among the three AI reviews that immediate replay while recording is active, exact decoded-frame stepping, dual-camera timing, replay/recording isolation, storage safety and licensing are unresolved technical risks requiring proof.
- It reiterates that a recording-list entry, a growing file, or a successful HTTP response does not prove immediate, user-visible replay from an actively written recording.
- It agrees that libmpv's backward-step function alone is not evidence of exactly one adjacent decoded frame per action. A controlled decode/positioning method with an explicit frame cache is one candidate, not a proven solution.
- It retains both advanced web/WebCodecs (A) and native/hybrid frame-cache (D) as serious candidates. Neither has reproducible project-specific proof. Architecture B using libmpv alone is not sufficient evidence for the exact-step requirement. Architecture C should remain lower priority pending measurement, not be declared impossible.
- It highlights the WebCodecs secure-context issue: LAN-IP HTTP origins may not qualify, while localhost is treated differently. It recommends testing the actual access paths, including localhost and LAN-IP, and HTTPS if required.
- It emphasizes that ingest-arrival timestamps must not be represented as camera capture timestamps. Alignment must report measured offset, drift and uncertainty.
- It warns that OS scheduling controls such as ionice are not a cross-platform isolation strategy, especially because ionice is not available on Windows.
- It calls for exact dependency/build/license review, including FFmpeg build/linkage, Qt bindings, codecs and redistribution obligations. General labels such as “free” or “LGPL” are not enough to establish that a particular distributed build meets project constraints.

### PoC plan refinement proposed by DeepSeek

DeepSeek recommends the following sequence:

1. **PoC 1 — Live-edge replay during active recording.** Use two disposable RTMP test streams and an active recording. Exercise 5/15/30-second look-back intervals, repeated replay requests, segment/part boundaries and an intentionally incomplete/truncated final part. Measure button-to-first-usable-frame latency, interval completeness/partial availability, file growth and recording continuity. The implementation must disclose unavailable portions honestly and must not block recording while replay reads.
2. **PoC 2 — Exact decoded-frame stepping.** Use footage with unique visible frame numbers at representative frame rates and GOP settings; include variable-frame-rate footage if relevant. Compare a WebCodecs client with a native libavcodec/decode-cache approach. Test repeated forward/backward steps, seeking and then stepping backward. Test WebCodecs from localhost and the intended LAN-IP origin (and HTTPS if needed). Record actual frame identities, skipped/repeated frames and action latency.
3. **PoC 4 — Replay/recording isolation.** First establish a dual-stream recording baseline, then repeat while performing replay, seeking, frame stepping and slow motion. Compare ingest/recording continuity, live latency, resource use and dropped frames.
4. **PoC 3 — Dual-camera timing.** Use repeated shared visible events at the beginning and end of a sustained run, while collecting available source, ingest and media timestamps. Measure offset, drift and uncertainty; introduce controlled network jitter/asymmetry where useful.
5. **PoC 5 — Storage safety.** Use a disposable test volume only. Exercise low-space warning, controlled stop, interrupted process and simulated abrupt failure. Verify preservation/playability of completed recordings, honest handling of the incomplete tail, and absence of automatic deletion.
6. **Launcher feasibility check.** Only after PoC 1 and PoC 2 have established a viable media path, test a portable launcher with relative paths and controlled start/stop on the target platforms. This is not an approval of a launcher technology.

DeepSeek proposes useful test instrumentation and scenarios, but suggested timings or other numerical targets in its report are not adopted as acceptance thresholds unless already specified in Draft 0.4 or subsequently approved by the owner. In particular, no unapproved sub-second latency or zero-drop numerical performance target is created by this log entry.

### ChatGPT's independent assessment and qualifications

- The PoC order **1, 2, 4, 3, 5, then launcher** is accepted as a practical risk-first sequence. PoC 4 is placed before timing and storage because replay/recording isolation is a core invariant that should be demonstrated before broader implementation.
- Comparing WebCodecs and native decode/cache approaches in PoC 2 is valuable, but the comparison must use the same source footage and equivalent test actions. The result must report the actual frame identities, not simply that a player API returned success.
- The localhost/LAN-IP/HTTPS test is a useful addition. The actual browser, OS, origin and codec combinations must be recorded; do not generalize from one browser or machine.
- DeepSeek's broad agreement summary should not be read as proof that the reviews were fully independent: the reviewers had overlapping project documents and conversation-derived material. The reports are useful independent perspectives, but independence of source material and testing is not established.
- Architecture D remains a working hypothesis, not a proven winner. A remains in consideration. B alone is not adequate unless a concrete implementation demonstrates exact stepping. C is lower priority, not conclusively disproven.
- The visible-flash/shared-event method is a test technique for measuring timing; it does not add a requirement for manual user calibration or hardware synchronization.
- The approved requirement for automatic two-camera alignment with measured offset/uncertainty remains in force. No requirement is added to claim capture-time synchronization where only ingest-time evidence exists.
- The exact one-decoded-frame stepping requirement remains firm. A PoC failure must be returned to the owner for a decision; it must not be silently weakened to approximate seeking.
- The approved bookmark model remains unchanged: bookmarks identify the viewed event/media moment, including when created from Instant Replay. This review does not reopen bookmark semantics.
- The storage policy remains unchanged: no automatic deletion; preserve existing recordings; use a test-derived safety reserve and controlled stop. All destructive storage tests must use disposable test data/volumes only.
- Any licensing statement about a codec, patent pool, FFmpeg build or GUI toolkit must be verified against the exact versions, build options, distribution model and applicable jurisdictions before selection. No unverified licensing claim is treated as clearance.

### Current disposition and next action

- **Status remains:** CONDITIONAL GO for isolated PoCs only; NO-GO for architecture lock and full Rev.9 implementation until critical feasibility evidence is available.
- The next practical task is to prepare and run **PoC 1** in the isolated Rev.9 development workspace, using only disposable test media and confirming the exact local test environment before relying on any binary or configuration.
- No local files, services, configurations or recordings were changed by this review. This log update does not claim that `C:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev9_Dev` has been inspected or populated.
- Production `D:\\JudoReplayKit` and Rev.8 test `D:\\JudoReplayKit_Rev8_Test` remain protected. No tests may modify either location.
- Requirements Draft 0.4 and DECISIONS.md are not changed by this entry.

## 2026-11-15 — FFmpeg static-linking and LGPL dependency-packaging gap identified

**Source:** Exact FFmpeg-Builds workflow/job logs and repository build scripts at recipe commit `c9fe5114060e507409f7ee07edfd82efaf25d767`; FFmpeg source licence/configuration at source commit `d838f9f9aeb0e09b4d068e2af20a46a12640696f`. Read-only source/log review. The candidate ZIP remains unextracted and unexecuted.

### Verified findings

- The exact Windows x64 LGPL 9.0 build log contains the actual linker command for the FFmpeg executables. It links external libraries statically; this is no longer merely an inference from the configure flags.
- The linker command includes `gmp` and `libaribb24`. FFmpeg's licence overview at the pinned source revision classifies both as LGPLv3 external libraries. The build uses `--enable-version3`, consistent with enabling LGPLv3-compatible components.
- The packaging script `variants/windows-install-static.sh` copies the generated binaries, FFmpeg documentation and presets into the package. The main build script copies the selected FFmpeg licence file (for this variant, `COPYING.LGPLv3`) to `LICENSE.txt`. The reviewed packaging path does not show a step that collects the full individual licence/notice texts for every statically linked external dependency, nor does it show provision of the corresponding dependency source and application/relink materials in the produced ZIP.
- LGPLv3 section 4(d)(0) describes an alternative compliance route for a combined work using a suitable shared-library mechanism: provide the Minimal Corresponding Source and Corresponding Application Code in a form suitable for recombination/relinking with a modified library. The exact obligations and available compliance route must be assessed for each applicable library and the actual distribution model; this log is a technical risk finding, not a legal opinion.
- The reviewed ZIP contents/build log identify FFmpeg executables, documentation, presets and the FFmpeg LGPLv3 licence text. The available evidence does not establish that the ZIP contains the source/relink materials or complete third-party licence/notice inventory needed to demonstrate compliance for all statically linked components.
- The absence of those materials from the reviewed packaging script is a material documentation/compliance gap. It does **not**, by itself, establish that the binaries are unlawful, malicious, or impossible to distribute under any compliant arrangement. Additional source, relink, notice and distribution evidence could change the assessment.
- The build's configure command does not show `--enable-gpl` or `--enable-nonfree`, and it explicitly disables several GPL-associated libraries. This remains a positive configuration finding, but does not resolve LGPL obligations for statically linked LGPL dependencies or the licences of every other linked component.

### Assessment / decision

- **Do not approve this candidate ZIP for bundling or distribution in JudoReplayKit at this stage.**
- The candidate archive remains checksum-matched to the publisher's release manifest, but its GitHub Artifact Attestation lookup returned HTTP 404; provenance is still unverified.
- Do not extract or execute the archive yet. Continue with a component-by-component inventory of every library present in the actual linker inputs/binaries: exact version/source, licence, required notices, source availability, static-link/relink obligations, and any separate patent or redistribution terms.
- Investigate a deliberately minimal FFmpeg build containing only the codecs, demuxers, muxers and protocols actually needed by the PoCs. A smaller build may reduce the dependency and licence-review surface, but it must still be verified from its actual configure/build/link logs and package contents.
- Obtain independent review of this evidence and the proposed compliance interpretation from DeepSeek and Mistral before changing the release/tooling decision.

- Build recipe revision: https://github.com/FFmpeg-Builds/FFmpeg-Builds/commit/c9fe5114060e507409f7ee07edfd82efaf25d767
